With the left hand one should bind the mudrā;
the right hand should hold the rosary.
As explained before, the secret one and the rest,
one should perform the recitation of the great wonder.
For what reason is recitation taught?
Diligence in virtue becomes victorious,
wonders too come to be seen,
the conduct of awakening becomes victorious,
and the play of the buddhas becomes manifest.
When, wearied by recitation, one rises,
one should bind the mudrās.
In order to act for the benefit of all sentient beings,
bowing to all the directions is taught.
One should read the secret sūtras and the rest
that bring attainment of the way of the awakening mind.
In order to generate the accumulation of merit,
one should properly worship the buddhas.
Then, at the time of the meal,
one should give a morsel offering in every way.
Having guarded it with the vidyā of the Buddha,
one should offer the food.
As the Buddha oneself, one should offer the food;
having divided it well, one should offer the food.
In the morning one should take the vow,
and that same at midday and at the junctures,
three times by day and three times by night.
The yoga that performs Buddha, vajra,
jewel and lotus:
one applies it from those four kinds.
The Buddha, the Dharma, the Saṅgha and awakening,
in six sessions by day and night;
vajra, bell, assembly and master,
likewise six for those who hold the vajra.
Material things, fearlessness and the Dharma,
and love, through every day and night as well:
the three dharmas and the four called Brahmā's,
one should recite in eight ways.
In its three great dharmas
one should always enter absorption:
the supreme secret, the supreme maṇḍala,
and likewise the performing of all actions.
Where it does not become wrongdoing,
one eats everything and accepts everything.
One should discard what is left over from the body,
and likewise remove all impurities.
Three times every day
one should bathe three times and take up the secret;
one should change one's clothes three times,
and three times put on clean clothes.
Meat, liquor, onions and the like,
foul-smelling belching from the mouth,
desire for sexual union and engaging in it,
and harming sentient beings: one should not delight in these.
Diligent, skilled in action, endowed with good conduct,
clever, gentle, joyful and clean,
doing the tasks and serving the guru,
one wears the ornament of virtue and goodness.
One should take the vows of the training
of both kinds, conventional and ultimate.
Eating little, one should perform the approach,
with the gaze turned inward and with faith
in mantras, mudrās, vidyā-mantras,
dhāraṇīs, the deity, the guru,
and the excellent emblems such as the vajra,
and possessed of a dwelling for the deity.
Not fearful, not deceitful or guileful,
not of unstable mind,
not delighting in nirvāṇa,
the mind not carried off by the senses,
the wise one does not delight in this world,
nor likewise does he delight in the world beyond.
One should not be indifferent to body and life,
and one should not transgress the training.
Not timid and not proud,
one should not desire worldly affairs.
In such a wondrous way as this
the yogin should practice.
Giving up idle talk, yet endowed with great eloquence,
giving up dependence, yet endowed with good gains,
heroic, steadfast and respectful to the guru,
fearing wrongdoing and restrained in every way,
one should be zealous as far as one is able.
Monthly, or else each year,
or twice in a single day,
likewise one should behold the guru.
Even one who commits the five inexpiable deeds,
abiding in such a way as this
and binding the eight kinds of mudrā,
will always attain accomplishment.
When the observance of pure conduct has been completed,
in the maṇḍala of the yoga ḍākinīs
the master, the Vajradhara,
will be empowered by all the buddhas.
He is the king, lord of all vidyās;
he knows the reality of all maṇḍalas,
he knows the reality of the rites of yoga,
and likewise he sees the families of the deities.
Having taken the vows, and being learned,
he will be empowered as a master of vidyā.
King who makes the great paths,
endowed with vidyā and knowing reality,
knowing what helps and what harms,
he holds sway over all, the gods and the rest.
Yakṣas, bhūtas, the lords of the gods and the rest,
the king of vidyā commands as servants.
He abides in the boat of the Dharma;
he worships the buddhas with excellent worship.
King of the host of bodhisattvas,
holder of the treasury, ever the holder of the secret,
he dwells in the great city of the wheel.
In him who holds the way of the bodhisattvas
the heart of the elixir of the true Dharma is born;
holding the essence, he worships the maṇḍala.
This one, who knows the way of the very intent
and knows the meaning of the way of the Great Vehicle,
with sublime worship surpassing offerings and gifts,
should make the secret maṇḍala.
At the very beginning
of the samādhi of the vajra wrathful one,
one should utter the fierce secret mantra
that surely destroys all obstructors,
and also the rite of recitation and fire offering
that is auspicious and destroys wrongdoing:
"I blaze like the vajra fire,
holding fierce, unbearable gnosis."
To perform the worship of the maṇḍala,
one should thoroughly examine the requisites;
or else, by one who performs it afterward,
they should be examined as is fitting.
It should not be made larger than a yojana,
nor smaller than six cubits.
This is called the twelve for accomplishment,
and there are also twelve for the performer.
If he is a king,
one should make it the extent of a yojana,
or else excellent all around
and fully endowed with four sides.
Or else, if the practitioner is a minister,
one of a thousand cubits is best;
or else, excellent all around,
it should not be made smaller than six cubits.
Or else, if it is some merchant,
or else a caravan leader,
it should be fully endowed with four sides
and not be made smaller than five.
Or else, if it is any others,
or if they are practitioners,
it should not be made smaller than ten,
fully endowed with four corners,
nor should it be made large.
Or else, if the practitioner is a master,
it should not be made smaller than six,
or else it should be of the measure of a yojana.

Or else it should be furnished with the requisites.
If the practitioner is a king,
it should be made of jewel powders;
it should be made well from ground jewels,
utterly excellent and delightful.
Indranīla, a deep blue,
should make the blue pigment;
padmarāga, of great hue,
supplies the red pigment;
the yellow pigment, of fine color,
is endowed with stainless rays;
and to have the white pigment,
the white should be made from crystal.
Vaiḍūrya and the color of madder:
these are taught as the five pigments.
If those are not to be had,
it should be made with a mixture of jewels.
Whether merchant or caravan leader,
it should be made with pigments.
Or else, if it is someone else,
other excellent pigments are prepared.
Even with ash, as the occasion allows,
masters may make it here.
Endowed with jewel powders,
or else endowed with the requisites:
if the practitioner is a king,
the various offerings should be to the measure of ten million,
or else endowed with a hundred thousand;
they should not be made fewer than a thousand.
Offerings and praises with incense and lamps,
vases of scented water,
likewise vasefuls of torma,
vases and lamps:
whether merchant or caravan leader,
they should not be made fewer than ten.
Even if some other person comes,
the foods to chew are ten thousand;
they should not be made fewer than ten.
The foods to eat are a hundred or a thousand;
they should not be made fewer than ten.
And all kinds of cooked dishes,
likewise every kind of scent,
music, graceful dance and play,
and likewise every excellence.
Then the vajra master,
having made offerings at that time,
and you, having gone forth,
should begin the observance in order to accomplish it.
Having bathed oneself and anointed the limbs,
wearing pure and clean clothes,
and dwelling in a secluded place,
one should invite all the buddhas.
Holding flowers and a censer in hand,
one should prostrate to all the directions.
The buddhas and all the rest,
endowed with the supreme gnosis of the buddhas, come;
one should bind the vajra mudrā
that summons the secret eye.
With the vajra mantra and mudrā
one should invite all the buddhas.
Then one should properly prepare the site,
secluded and wholly free of distraction:
a mountain or a wilderness,
or likewise the inside of a cave,
or a place at the foot of a tree,
the bank of a river or else a shore.
When one dwells in a place of accomplishment,
the deities will swiftly come.
Ground with stones, gravel and bones,
with ash, refuse and filth,
rough, pervaded with roots and the like,
one should avoid for drawing it.
Very good, of fine luster and smooth,
of proper measure and free of gravel:
to the depth of a fathom or a cubit
one should purify the site of the maṇḍala.
In villages and districts,
in cities and in provinces,
in royal palaces and in marketplaces,
the great maṇḍala is not to be made.
Having prepared such ground,
one should prepare the upper part,
a fathom or else a cubit,
fitted with four steps.
Having purified it, then in the center
of the site one should plant a kīla.
With the mantra of the vajra kīla
and with the mudrā of the vajra kīla
one should beautify the ground of worship.
It is trodden by one who acts as a vidyādhara.
With the mudrā that binds obstructors
one should bind all the obstructors everywhere.
The directions of Fire, of Yama, of Water,
of Wind and of Indra, the vajra directions,
one should purify with the mudrā of the unbearable vajra
and with the vajra mantra.
For a site of worship the measure of a yojana,
one should begin from the fifteenth day;
on the second or the third,
there will be no fault in the lying down.
If one lacks the means,
one should apply oneself for about a month:
beginning from the fifteenth day,
one should complete it on the fifteenth.
If for about half a month,
one should purify it on the fifteenth.
If one is in haste, from the eighth day
one should complete it on the fifteenth.
On the ninth and the tenth,
the thirteenth and the fourteenth,
the seventh and the fifth,
there will be no fault in the lying down.
If one begins on that very day,
one should complete it on that same day.
In order to perform the great yoga,
one should prepare the ground of yoga.
Having bathed oneself and anointed the limbs,
wearing garments of various kinds,
and decked with every ornament,
the vajra master is beautifully adorned.
Clean and wearing clean clothes,
together with pure companions,
fully devoted to the welfare of all sentient beings,
one should properly take up the awakening mind.
Even for a hundred thousand eons,
however much they may strive,
four kinds of person in the world,
though they practice, will not attain accomplishment:
those who have not generated the mind,
the faithless, who will not attain,
those who hold to doubt,
and those who do not carry out the commands.

Then that site of the maṇḍala,
well smeared with scented water,
should be sprinkled in all directions
with water that is scented.
Having purified it well with cow dung
and anointed it well with what is scented,
one should then prepare
clean scents such as saffron.
With the mudrā of the vajra maṇḍala
one should properly bless the maṇḍala;
with the mudrā of the Vajra Eye
it is looked upon by those with lotus eyes.
In the middle of the maṇḍala and at its end
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering.
The rite of the fire offering is of five kinds;
one should apply the rite of the fire offering.
For the rite of the accomplishment of the vidyā
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering,
likewise the collection of mantras and mudrās,
and not mantras and mudrās of that sort.
First one should destroy the obstructors;
then one should properly begin the fire offering.
After that, one should draw completely
the maṇḍala of the great secret worship.
The fire offering is taught as five kinds,
or else it becomes sixteen.
Each according to its own rite,
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering.
In order to accomplish whatever is intended,
one properly begins whatever worship it may be.
If one is unable, the greatly fortunate one
should prepare the food;
mixed with what is scented,
or else one should eat the three white substances.
In this way the king, Vajradhara,
will bestow his blessing.

I shall explain the sublime binding,
my own, which surely destroys the obstructors,
the excellent action of the master,
the supreme means of repelling the obstructors.
Always one should salute Vajrakrodha
and Vajrasattva.
With the mudrā of Vajrāṅkuśa
one should summon all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra noose
one should draw in all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of Vajrakarma
one summons all the obstructors everywhere.
"There is no wrongdoing, so do not fear!
Nor is there any harming of sentient beings.
Obstructors arise from one's own conceptual thought,
just as nonvirtue arises from ignorance."
With the mudrā of the vajra chain
one should bind all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra fence
one should make all things of vajra nature.
With the mudrā of Vajrayakṣa
one should torment all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra tether
one should lead off all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra casting down
one casts down all the obstructors everywhere.
With the mudrā of the vajra striking
one should strike all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra blaze
one should set all the obstructors ablaze.
With the mudrā of the vajra sword
one should cut all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra crown
one should pound all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra wheel
one should demolish all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra machine
one should crush all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra khaṭvāṅga
one should chop up all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra short spear
one should pierce all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra burner
one should burn all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra whirler
one should whirl all the obstructors around.
With the mudrā of the vajra thread
one should shake all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra repeller
one utterly destroys all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra destroyer
one should crush all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra terrifier
one terrifies all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra eater
one should eat all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra chewer
one should chew all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra tearing
one should tear apart all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra feller
one should fell all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra raising
one should raise up all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra killing
one should kill all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra reviver
one should revive all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra accomplishing
one properly brings all the obstructors under control.
With the mudrā of the vajra sealing
one should seal all the vicious ones.
With the mudrā of the vajra wind
one sends all the obstructors off on the wind.
With the mudrā of the vajra water
one scatters all the obstructors in the water.
With the vajra fire, utterly keen,
one should burn all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra stupefier
one makes all the obstructors faint.
With the mudrā of Vajrahūṃkara
one should offer all the obstructors into the fire.
With the mudrā of the vajra gathering
one should seize all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra fang
one should bite all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra piercing
one should pierce all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra destroyer
one should demolish all the obstructors.
With the vajra that carries out commands,
having summoned them, one should set them under command.
With the mudrā of the vajra threatening finger
one should threaten all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the vajra splitter
one should split all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of Vajrahūṃkara
one should destroy every class of obstructor.
With the unbearable Vajrayakṣa,
with the vajra blazing like fire,
and with the mudrā of the vajra terrifier,
one should strike them with the vajra foot.
With the mudrā that destroys obstructors
one should then destroy the obstructors above.

From the great secret tantra of the vajra,
supreme of the sublime, the Supreme Secret,
one should enter the absorption of the vajra wrathful one.
A hundred and ten and more,
and the secret of the vajra master,
are fully taught in the extensive version.
They were compiled in order to punish
all the vicious ones, the obstructors and the rest.
In order to accomplish the great maṇḍala
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering.
Eight kinds and ten more,
the secret of the vajra master:
this is the great secret tantra;
it is fully explained in that tantra.
Together with the rites of the secret fire offering,
I shall further explain the first.
In the secret action of the great yoga
one does not interrupt the rite of the fire offering.
In this great secret tantra
that compiles all the rites,
everything is summed up
in twenty kinds of fire offering:
pacifying and enriching,
the rite of the fierce rite,
prolonging life and summoning,
increasing wealth and grain,
protection and non-protection and obstructors,
utterly destroying all wrongdoing,
binding and killing and wrath,
passion and subduing,
bringing under control, and beauty of form.

appearing in any form one wishes,
going in the sky, and accomplishing
the substances of accomplishment:
for these, the vajra master
who makes the maṇḍala of accomplishment should perform it.
Of such kinds as these,
twenty excellent fire offerings are taught.
In accordance with the rite of the fire offering,
first the hearth is to be made.
For enriching it is square;
for pacifying it is round.
For summoning,
one should make it in the shape of a vajra,
and likewise another in the shape of a lotus.
For the fierce rite it is triangular.
In order to destroy all wrongdoing,
it is round, furnished with porticoes.
For increasing the lineage it is shaped like a jewel.
For increasing life and for diminishing it,
and for utter purity, it has the shape of a mudrā.
For protection it is like armor.
For binding it is encircled by a moat.
For killing it is furnished with fangs.
For assuming any form one wishes, any shape will do.
For invisibility it is furnished with a bow.
For the directions and for flying in the sky, a hook.
For accomplishing the mudrā that carries out commands,
and for accomplishing the great maṇḍala,
it is a wheel or else a square.
For accomplishing substances and medicines,
the hearth resembles the organ of a woman.
Or else one should properly draw
the maṇḍala of the common accomplishments.
Just as the wheel of the maṇḍala is,
so the hearth of the fire offering becomes alike.
Thus one is endowed with the excellent accomplishment of a vidyādhara,
and everything becomes perfect.
A little beyond the wheel and the rest,
one should dig the hearth of the fire offering.
These are the twenty hearths.

In all the rites of the maṇḍala,
by merely seeing whichever hearth it is,
one will be freed from all wrongdoing.
I shall explain the teaching on the hearth.
First the lines are to be laid down,
and one should draw the ground well with colors.
Square, with what are called the eight lines,
one should draw out the central wheel.
The parts of the square are equal,
well divided and well drawn,
fully endowed with four porticoes,
beautifully adorned with the door recesses,
fully endowed with four doors;
the doors should have eight parts.
It is adorned with nets and half-moons,
the vajra door recesses bearing vajras,
with shoots of a lotus grove
and with garlands winding all around.
The great mudrā of the ladle
one should draw in the eastern part.
A trident and a half-moon,
a sword, a bull and a lovely banner,
and the great mudrā of bliss called desire,
one should draw in the southern direction.
A wheel, a conch and a club,
a horse, an elephant and a boar:
these mudrās one should draw in the west.
A short spear, a bell and a banner,
a peacock and likewise a goat,
and a cock poised to fight,
one should draw in the northern direction.
On its wheel of commitment
one should properly draw the mudrā of the Buddha.
In the central place there is a crown,
adorned on the outside with vajras.
A blazing vajra, ablaze,
is in the eastern part;
in the south, various jewels;
in the west, a lotus vajra;
in the north, Vajrahūṃkara,
who destroys all the vicious ones.
The buddhas, the vajras, the great means,
are outside, as sixteen mudrās.
Over whatever hearth has been dug,
one should visualize a canopy.
With flowers, firewood, incense and the rest,
one should worship the maṇḍala as prescribed.
Having bathed oneself and anointed the limbs,
wearing various garments and ornaments,
beautifully decked with every ornament,
one is empowered with excellent scents:
"I am the king, the lotus holder,
with the Tathāgata in the middle of my topknot."
Or else as Vajrasattva,
or endowed with the empowerment of Vajrasattva,
together with a lovely woman,
clasping her tightly in one's arms,
with the organ fully erect,
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering:
"How wonderful! Bliss, great bliss,
the unsurpassed bliss of passion!
Vajrasattva, great passion,
lord of the world, grant me accomplishment!"
Recollecting the bliss of passion,
one should not indulge the passion of desire.
Having drawn up the element of the woman,
one should give up the seven kinds of intercourse.
With the organ fully risen,
bringing the organ close,
and recollecting passion,
one should properly begin the rite of the fire offering.
The organ is called the hand emblem;
its rising is to be heightened.
Even more than the hand emblem of the vajra holder,
the wise one should hold to it.
The awakening mind of the vajra holder
and of the bodhisattva is steadfast,
and marked by the six perfections.
Through love, passion bestows accomplishment;
it shows joy in wisdom and means.
It is always to be relied on.
With the organ fully risen,
one should rely on it all the more.
Whatever lovely vidyā consort one longs for,
the yogin should kiss her.
When, with intensity, she has become the great mudrā,
he should clasp her tightly in his arms.
Whatever abundant perfect joy there is,
that is held to be the ground of Perfect Joy.
That to which one is attached, well examined,
one should savor again and again.
Anointed fully with compassion and loving-kindness,
covered with the garment of modesty,
anointed fully with discipline and the observance,
adorned with the mind of awakening,
one endowed with such an observance
should rely on the great pigment.
Moreover the outer limbs,
since the vajra holder holds them,
in accordance with the rite of the fire offering,
one should perform the fire offering facing east.
Just as it is stated in the scripture,
the vajra master should perform it.
For pacifying, the supreme awakening of the Buddha;
for enriching, by Vajraratna;
Vajrakrodha for the fierce rite;
summoning is taught to be by the hook;
for the extraction of essences, the vajra medicine;
likewise for increasing life, the great seal
of the vajra medicine of Acala;
the vajra peak for increasing wealth;
for protection, the rite of the wheel;
for repelling, one should hold a vajra;
with the great mudrā of the chain in the left hand
the destroying of wrongdoing is taught;
for binding, the vajra binding;
for killing, the vajra killer;
for passion, by Vajrarāga,
and it is also taught for subduing;
for bringing under control, by the vajra of control.

For assuming any form one wishes, the Viśva mudrā;
for the accomplishment of going through the sky,
by the magical display of vajra accomplishment.
By the great mudrā of the lotus medicine
comes the supreme bliss of accomplishment through substances.
For the accomplishment of the complete maṇḍala,
by the mudrā of the vajra maṇḍala,
one should take up that supreme mudrā
of the king, lord of all knowledge mantras (vidyā).
For accomplishing all accomplishments,
by the supreme medicine of the great lotus,
bind the mudrā with the left hand
and perform the fire offering with the right.
By the mudrā of the crossed vajra
the act of fire offering should be sealed.
The left, with hūṃ, makes the threatening forefinger;
the right bares the fangs;
with the mudrā of the fierce rite
bind the directions with powdered bone.
oṃ suṃbhani suṃbha hūṃ gṛhṇa grahṇa hūṃ gṛdṇābaya gṛhṇāpaya hūṃ ānaya ho bagapan bihyārāja hūṃ phaṭ

The left joined with the gesture of teaching the Dharma,
with the right perform the fire offering.
Through absorption in the Dharma of the buddhas,
by burning sesame, wrongdoing is burned away.
oṃ sarva pāpaṃ dahana vajrāya svāhā

The left hand becomes the jewel;
with the right perform the fire offering.
With the seven grains, or else with jewels,
one should give delight with every requisite.
oṃ maṇi ratna kramakāya svāhā

Deeply settled in meditative equipoise,
kindle the fire with sweet wood;
if the yogin burns rice in it,
the household will surely prosper.
oṃ vajra puṣṭaye svāhā

With firewood that is very sweet
kindle the fire well;
if one makes the fire offering with kuśa sprouts and sesame,
it is supreme for the purpose of protection.
oṃ vajra apratihata vajraya svāhā

Sweet kindling sticks,
and likewise sprouts of dūrvā grass,
smeared with butter, burned with the vajra mudrā,
are supreme for increasing life.
oṃ vajra āyuṣe svāhā

With pungent firewood,
in the concentration of the vajra lord of wrath,
kindle the fire in the hearth
and summon with thorns.
oṃ vajra ākarṣaya svāhā

Likewise with every kind of wood
the wise one kindles the fire;
by constantly offering lotuses in the fire
vajra binding comes about.
oṃ vajra bandhaya paṃ

Likewise by the rite of application
the wise one should burn kindling sticks;
by making a fire offering of bitter fruits
one kills in an instant.
oṃ vajra māranaye hūṃ

Likewise, burning red pākṣa
brings under one's control.
oṃ vajra rāganaye hrīḥ

Making a fire offering with śapuṣpa
is likewise supreme for arousing passion.
oṃ vajra śaṃkarāya svāhā

If, in wrath, one burns the rice of desire,
one obtains whatever form one wishes.
oṃ vajra kāmarūpaṇi svāhā oṃ vajra kharacireṇi svāhā

With firewood of bitter taste,
kindle the fire in great wrath;
burning vajra flowers in wrath,
they will do as the vajra commands.
hūṃ vajra śaṃkarāya svāhā oṃ bhuta vaśaṃkarāya svāhā

Following the rite for all accomplishments,
the wise one should offer white mustard seed in the fire.
Since white mustard seed is supremely auspicious,
it is the supreme fire offering for accomplishment.
oṃ sarva siddhaye svāhā

To accomplish the state of a knowledge holder (vidyādhara), the wise one should burn svastikas; by applying the maṇḍala of accomplishment, the fire offering is made in the vajra maṇḍala. oṃ siddha siddhaye svāhā oṃ dala dhipataye svāhā maṇḍal adhipataye svāhā oṃ bhuḥ svāhā oṃ bhuva svāhā oṃ sva svāhā oṃ bhur bhuva svaḥ svahā oṃ svasti svāhā oṃ svasti namaḥ svāhā oṃ siddhe prabhātaye svāhā oṃ sarva duḥkh upaśamane svāhā oṃ vajra siddhaye svāhā oṃ kārya kāraṇi puṣṭa śantike svāhā

The syllable phaṭ is for the fierce rite;
by the syllable jaḥ summoning comes about;
the syllable paṃ is applied for binding;
for bringing under control, the syllable śrī.
The syllable hūṃ is applied for threatening,
and for killing, too, the syllable hūṃ;
ha is for excellent protection;
the syllable hūṃ is for repelling;
with śrī one should accomplish various forms;
oṃ is taught for invisibility;
ho is for passion and for summoning;
u holds the purity of the secret;
for one engaged in the fierce rite, the syllable hūṃ;
hūṃ accomplishes miraculous powers;
for accomplishing the great goddess, ā;
oṃ is to be applied to all without fail.

Yellow kindling sticks are for increase,
white ones for pacifying,
red ones for bringing under control,
and black ones for the act of the fierce rite.
For pacifying they are smeared with butter,
for increase, with milk,
for bringing under control, with salt,
and for the fierce rite, with pungent spices.
Through these vast distinctions
the acts of fire offering should be understood.
These twenty fire offerings of reality
are the secret of Vajradhara.

This is the secret of fire offering called The Twenty Principles.

Then, the great means of accomplishment:
in the maṇḍala, wishing to accomplish everything,
by the rite of applying accomplishment,
endowed with the application of the whole host of accomplishments,
the great vajra master,
born into a steadfast family,
abiding truly in the Great Vehicle,
riding the great chariot of the Dharma
until the end of countless eons,
truly endowed with wisdom and skillful means,
striving for the benefit of all sentient beings,
embraced by all the buddhas,
abiding in the way of a great master,
having gone supremely to the end of the grounds,
born into the family of all the buddhas,
the vajra master of great austerity,
having dispelled all obstructors,
being properly established in the site of the maṇḍala,
and having performed the rite of fire offering,
should undertake the great gift of the Dharma.
Arisen from the very essence of the heart of the true Dharma,
it is the unsurpassed worship of the buddhas,
the magical display of the bodhisattvas;
it brings attainment of the way of wisdom and skillful means.
The great victor, great wisdom,
and, through skillful means, the marvelous nature of reality.
Like a brahmin, born of a supreme family,
he holds the great secret of the bodhisattvas.
If he is able, the greatly fortunate one,
from the time he begins the worship
until it is completed,
should not eat.

Or else one may eat the three white substances
well mixed together with scents,
for then King Vajradhara
will bestow his blessing.
But if the patient one does not eat
by day or by night,
then the food that is the very nature of the Dharma
will satisfy him, without a doubt.

The maṇḍalas should be purified
and prepared with every kind of scent;
with water cleansed by mantra
one should sprinkle everything all around.
When one performs the worship,
one should pay homage to all the directions,
and having performed in due order the acts
of confession and supplication,
clouds of offerings, rejoicing,
the offering of oneself, and so forth,
in the very center of the great maṇḍala
one should sit in the sattva posture.
Ten kinds of seated posture are taught
that comprise all the maṇḍalas.
This supreme great secret, reality itself,
is the worship of all the maṇḍalas:
the glorious, the auspicious, the threatening forefinger,
likewise the right half-crossed posture,
the outward-extended and the seated,
and likewise the jewel nature;
these are taught as ten in nature.
One hundred and ten postures
are fully taught in that tantra,
the secret of the secret tantras.

Seated cross-legged in the sattva posture,
with mind fixed upon the buddhas,
recollecting great loving-kindness
for the three worlds, moving and unmoving,
the yogin should enter into absorption
in the supreme samādhi of the initial union.
Then comes the supreme king of maṇḍalas,
and then again the king of action.
From the excellent higher samādhi
they are taught as a hundred and ten;
for all actions they are stated as three;
this is fully taught in the secret tantra.
Without the application of samādhi
the maṇḍala does not become stable;
without the application of the maṇḍala
accomplishment itself does not arise.
Therefore, always and with effort,
one should practice samādhi, accomplishment and maṇḍala.

If you wish for the Supreme Secret,
the secret of the unsurpassed vehicle,
then I shall properly explain
the domain of the three samādhis.
Ordinary people are not capable
of the magical display of the bodhisattvas;
their supreme samādhi
must be properly performed by the mind itself.
As explained in the first tantra,
the magical display of the buddhas is taught.
Through the samādhi of a bodhisattva
the intelligent one should imagine himself with great miraculous power,
and should imagine that he himself
has performed various fierce austerities.
He should imagine the abode of the gods,
the abode called Akaniṣṭha,
and imagine the pure assembly
in which all the buddhas are gathered.
First paying homage to the buddhas,
and then likewise to the sages,
having received their command as taught,
he should again sit cross-legged.
He should imagine the fivefold full awakening
that delights all.
Himself in the body of a buddha
adorned with all the marks,
and empowered by all the buddhas:
these five he should contemplate in himself.
He should dwell in the vajra seat
and imagine the lofty peak.

This is the samādhi called the Initial Union.

Dwelling in the vajra east,
the wheel of the buddhas is magically displayed;
the bodhisattvas too are sent forth,
and the maṇḍala of the wheel is emanated:
the family maṇḍala together with its king,
the gatekeepers, the graceful goddesses and the rest;
together with the goddesses,
through the application of the mudrās,
one empowers the vajra holder
and properly performs it as the vajra action.
In the space of one's own sphere
one should visualize with devotion all the buddhas,
and properly confer empowerment
as the very nature of all the buddhas.
As the king who holds the vajra Dharma
one should examine the arrangement of the maṇḍala,
summon all the buddhas,
and make the vajra holders enter supremely.
Accomplishing and bringing under control:
this act is supreme.
In the maṇḍala of one's own family
one should especially visualize that place:
the wheel as it is, the arrangement as it is,
the mudrās as they are, and the empowerment conferred.

This is the samādhi called the Supreme King of Action.

Having bathed oneself and anointed the limbs,
wearing various ornaments and garments,
armlets and bracelets,
likewise rings and a crown,
with gold thread and pearls
on the hands, the feet and then the neck,
so that the whole body is adorned;
likewise one should put on fine garments,
especially of silk, and be purified.
Or else one wears the Dharma robes,
like the banner that is the seal of the Buddha.
If one is a monk with shaven head,
one should bind up long, coiled hair
of a blue color, dark blue;
or else hair grown on one's own head.
One should be oneself Vajrasattva,
or else act as Vajrakarma.
Without the secret that is the nature of yoga
excellent accomplishment will not be obtained.

Having sat as is fitting,
evenly in the center of the maṇḍala,
rising up and facing east,
without pride and without error,
one circumambulates as Vajrasattva.
The excellent hundred and eight names
should be recited once.
Having offered praise, one oneself
should properly apply the accomplishment.
Through the application of great compassion
I should exhort the sage:
"Vajrasattva, great being,
supreme lord of unfailing purpose,
the best of those born of the supreme Great Vehicle,
bodhisattva, accomplish me!
Great being, great means,
vajra wheel, great worship,
great accomplishment, great miraculous power,
Vajrasattva, accomplish me!"
One should properly take up the vajra name
and properly circumambulate in all directions,
gaze upon the complete maṇḍala,
and recollect the vajra yoga.
Again seated cross-legged:
"I myself am the wheel-turning monarch."
In binding the host of mudrās
one should do no fewer than a thousand.

At the very least, one should take up
the mudrā of Vajrasattva himself.
Rising, one should speak these words:
"I am Vajrasattva,
who fully accomplishes the great maṇḍala.
Vajra and all the rest, come!
I am King Vajradhara;
accomplish me as Vajrasattva!"

With a cord dyed in rich colors and scented,
especially tightly twisted,
with the great mudrā of the vajra cord
one should lay out the lines of the great maṇḍala.
The wheel as it is, the arrangement as it is,
following the text exactly,
one should draw the wheel of the maṇḍala as it is
and lay down the vajra lines completely.
Then its own family secret
fully accomplishes the great wheel.
The wheel of the great maṇḍala
I shall now explain in due order.

It is square, with four doors,
made beautiful by four arches,
properly furnished with four lines,
and adorned with silk streamers and garlands.
All the corner sections,
and the corners of the door frames,
are adorned with vajra jewels.
One should lay out the lines of the outer maṇḍala.
Its inner level
should be drawn like a wheel,
encircled by a vajra garland
and beautified by eight pillars,
adorned with five maṇḍalas
placed before the vajra pillars.
Each maṇḍala should be distinguished
as it lies outside and inside.
In the very center of the maṇḍala,
where the five maṇḍalas are drawn,
adorned with eight supreme pillars,
is what is taught as the fourfold maṇḍala.

Going to the very center of the center,
one should place the form of the Buddha:
the great king who holds the Dharma,
Vairocana, the great all-pervading lord.
In all the directions around the Buddha,
in the centers of the maṇḍalas,
the four supreme samayas too
should be properly drawn in due order.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
is placed before and behind;
Vajrāṅkuśa on the right side;
on the western side, the crusher of Māra,
Vajrasādhu, the great king;
the great holder of the Dharma, endowed with power;
the holder of the Dharma is in the north;
and in front, the vajra in the attitude of pride.
For this, its secret heart mantra,
supreme in bringing about true understanding,
fully freeing from all suffering,
fully urging on the mind of awakening:
hūṃ jaḥ ho sā

In the maṇḍala of the eastern quarter,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in the center of that maṇḍala
one should place Akṣobhya.
The heart mantra of vajra speed,
preceded by the cutting of doubt,
excellent, with the assembly going before,
fully destroys all wrongdoing:
oṃ vegakrama ho
Great secret of all the buddhas,
the Sage, supreme among beings, Akṣobhya,
abiding on the elephant seal,
holds the mudrā of peace.
For him the heart mantra is this: hūṃ

In all of its directions
the supreme vajra deities are placed,
adorned with every ornament,
with wrathful faces yet smiling:
Vajrasattva, the great king;
Vajrāṅkuśa, the great teacher;
Vajra Desire, the great Māra;
Vajrasādhu, of great miraculous power.
One should place Vajraratna;
what is taught as Vajrarāga,
one should place as Vajradharma;
what is taught as Vajrasādhu,
one should take up as Vajrakarma.
The fourfold samaya:
one should place the wheel-turning monarchs,
all wearing the crowns of buddhas,
empowered as wheel-turning monarchs:
Vajraratna, likewise Karma,
Padma, and the various, of great joy.
By the Blessed One Vairocana
that supreme awakening was properly taken up.
The remaining transformations of his limbs,
Vajrasattva and the others, should be placed.

Then, having set down the buddha family,
for the heart of the vajra family
the mudrā and the heart mantra are taught:
one should take up the vajra mudrā.
hūṃ jaḥ ho sva
In teaching the syllables ja and the rest,
the syllable oṃ is properly placed;
ho, dhi, the dharmadhātu syllable a;
the syllable sva is transformed.

Going south with vajra speed,
to the maṇḍala wheel of Ratnasambhava,
the supreme Blessed One holds it fully.
In all of its directions
one should draw Vajraratna and the rest:
jewel splendor, the banner,
and the one adorned with jewel teeth.
For these, the heart mantras are:
oṃ aḥ trā ha

In the maṇḍala of the western quarter,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center Vajradharma,
Amitābha, should be placed.
His heart mantra is hrīḥ.
In all of its directions
one should draw Vajrapadma and the rest:
the sharp lotus, likewise the cause,
and speech, with the lotus mudrā.
For these, the heart mantras are hrīḥ rdaṃ maṃ raṃ

Going with vajra speed
into the center of the northern maṇḍala,
the Blessed One Amoghasiddhi,
the Buddha himself, should be placed.
His heart mantra is kaṃ.
In all of its directions
one should draw Vajrakarma and the rest:
the protection of all, the great yakṣa,
action, and the very firm fist.
For these, the heart mantras are kaṃ haṃ hūṃ paṃ

In the four corners of the central wheel
one should properly draw the four goddesses:
Vajra, Ratna, likewise Karma,
and Padma are taught as the four goddesses.
For them, the heart mantras are oṃ oṃ hrīḥ a

This completes the central maṇḍala of the first section, from the great maṇḍala that is king of all.

Having completed the central maṇḍala,
one should draw all the mudrās.
Those mudrās are blessed;
one should take up the vajra mudrā.
From the center, the supreme, in supreme awakening;
the auspicious is the mudrā of the east;
in the southern quarter, supreme giving;
in the west, holding the wheel of Dharma;
in the north, that called granting fearlessness,
held by Amoghasiddhi.
The remaining mudrās with their hand emblems.
Then one should draw the host of vajra beings.

This completes the section called the center of the great vajra wheel, from the great maṇḍala, the king of all maṇḍalas.

Having gone with vajra speed
to the maṇḍala of the eastern quarter,
one should properly draw the five maṇḍalas
of the delightful great vajra family.
With eight pillars, great vajras,
adorned with five maṇḍalas,
one should set out the vajra wheel
furnished with four maṇḍalas.
In the center of its eastern part
one should properly draw one in the form of a buddha:
a blue utpala, endowed with great light,
with a wrathful face yet smiling,
fierce, encircled by a garland of flames,
holding the vajra tiriṃtiri,
the wrathful one holding the vajra gaze,
who fully tames all the violent.
Before the Buddha, Vajradhara
should be placed in the midst of the blaze;
likewise in the midst of the blaze, Padma,
and likewise Ratna, with their various emblems.
That is the great wheel of the east.

Having gone to the eastern section,
in the center of its maṇḍala
one should properly draw Vajrahūṃkara:
four-faced and eight-armed,
he holds the eight emblems,
his fangs slightly bared,
with a wrathful face yet smiling.
Hook, vajra, chain,
and noose are taught as four emblems;
he holds arrow and bow as two;
two are gathered by the syllable hūṃ.
With his left foot
he should press down Maheśvara;
with his right he presses the goddess Umā
down to between her breasts.
He stands well in the left-extended posture,
encircled by a garland of flames, radiant,
his body blue in color, fierce,
striking fear into all the buddhas.
In all of its directions
one should place the Vajrakrodhas,
all encircled by wrathful blaze,
their fangs slightly bared:
the wrathful Sattva, the wrathful vajra,
likewise the wrathful Rāga,
the wrathful Sādhu, the fierce;
they should encircle the central maṇḍala.

Having gone with vajra speed,
on the right side of the king of the wrathful ones,
surrounded by the great wrathful beings,
in the center is the frowning wrathful one.
In all of its directions,
endowed with the empowerment of the Vajrakrodhas,
one should draw them all and properly place them.
In the supreme maṇḍala of the frowning one,
in all of its directions,
one should place the jewel wrathful ones:
the frowning one, the Sun, the banner,
and the wrathful Hāsa, wholly arisen.

In the third excellent maṇḍala,
having gone with vajra speed,
surrounded by the great wrathful beings,
one should properly draw the vajra division.
In all of its directions
one should place the lotus wrathful ones:
Dharma, the sharp one, likewise the cause,
and Hāsa, the wrathful one of great miraculous power.

Having gone with vajra speed
to the great supreme family of action,
one should properly draw the great-souled king of action,
the vajra descent.
In all of its directions
one should draw the wrathful one of action and the rest:
the action, the yakṣa, the rākṣasa,
and the great fist, the miraculous power of gathering.
One should draw all five maṇḍalas;
this is taught as the vajra family.
It is called the great, great maṇḍala,
the secret itself, the lord of the assembly.

Then the heart mantra of them all,
delightful to the whole vajra family:
here the syllable hūṃ is spoken.
The fierce one proclaims ha ha, hūṃ;
proclaiming hūṃ hūṃ, he strikes terror.
The king of its maṇḍala
should be made the well-gathered vajra secret.
In the corners of the wheel maṇḍala
one should properly draw the four goddesses:
the wrathful Sattvī, Ratna,
Padma, and Karma, it should be known.
At all the borders
those with mudrās and samaya are held.

This completes the second section of the king of all maṇḍalas. The maṇḍala of the eastern section is also complete.

In the maṇḍala outside the pillars
of the wheel of the central maṇḍala:
the graceful one, the garland, likewise song,
and the dance goddess, with their mudrās.
For the eastern vajra family itself,
in the outer place, likewise.
The four generated upon the sun
are held by the vajra goddesses.
In the arising of the application of the vajra maṇḍala,
the syllable hūṃ is placed everywhere.
Likewise for the king of the buddha family
the syllable hūṃ is spoken here.
The mudrās are transformations of one's own limbs;
one should take up the vajra mudrā.
Through fully applying all the mudrās,
they are secret, and the supreme secret is taught.

This completes the section called the Great Vajra Family, from the great maṇḍala called the Supreme Secret, the king of all maṇḍalas.

In the southern jewel maṇḍala,
having gone there with vajra speed,
one should place there the five wheels
of the perfect maṇḍala.
In the wheel of its maṇḍala
one should place the form of a buddha:
the Great Jewel, riding a horse,
the Buddha Sun, the Tathāgata,
his body encircled by a garland of flames,
wholly pervaded by garlands of light rays,
holding a supreme garland of jewels,
adorned with every ornament,
laughing with a row of teeth,
having the mudrā of the row of teeth,
holding a banner at the tip of his thumb.
In the center of the sun, the great Buddha,
his body of nectar like the moon,
Vajraratna, of great good fortune,
supreme in the family of the vajra sun.
In all of its directions:
the jewel Sattva, Padma,
and likewise Vajrakarma.

adorned with every ornament,
beautifully arrayed as a wheel-turning monarch,
arisen from the great jewel family,
the jewel vajra holding the sun.
Then the heart mantra is trāṃ.

In the eastern maṇḍala of the south,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center one should properly draw
the great being, Great Jewel Light,
adorned with every ornament,
endowed with the vajra empowerment of the buddhas.
In all of its directions
one should likewise properly draw the jewels:
the jewel Sattva, likewise Rāga,
and the king Sādhu, of great miraculous power;
the mudrās of Vajraratna and the rest,
their own mudrās with their emblems.

Having gone with vajra speed,
and settling upon the southern place,
in the excellent great jewel family
one should draw the excellent Vajraratna,
endowed with the empowerment of the four jewels,
supreme, bearing the buddhas upon his crown,
adorned with every ornament,
endowed with the empowerment of the jewel garland.
In all of its directions
one should properly draw the four beings:
the Jewel King, the banner,
Hāsa, and the great Jewel King.

Having gone with vajra speed
to the western vajra maṇḍala:
the great-souled Padma Ratna,
endowed with empowerment by the jewel garland,
holding the supreme jewel lotus,
the great being with the crown of the buddhas,
fully holding jewel peace,
with sidelong glances, his eyes impassioned with desire,
gazing upon the persons to be tamed,
the great king who holds the lotus,
Lokeśvara Amitāyus.
In all of its directions
one should properly draw the jewel lotus:
likewise the jewel lotus,
likewise the cause, and the one holding speech.

Having gone with vajra speed
to the maṇḍala of the northern quarter,
one should properly draw, with great nobility,
the king of the great jewel action:
the Blessed One, lord of sages, Amoghasiddhi,
the buddha of magical display who holds all actions,
Vajrakarma, the king who holds the treasury,
the supreme and excellent chief of wheel-turning monarchs,
with the crown of the buddhas, endowed with the four actions,
his limbs well adorned with every ornament,
sealed with the excellent jewel of action,
having become supreme in the great assembly of the jewel maṇḍala.
In all of its directions
one should properly draw the action jewels:
the action, the rākṣasa, the yakṣa,
and the one holding its fist of gathering.
Then the heart mantra of them all
is trāṃ.
The yogin of the great jewel family
should recite the syllable trāṃ.
As is fitting, one should properly draw
one's own mudrās and emblems.

This completes the third section of the great maṇḍala, the king of all maṇḍalas. The maṇḍala of the southern section is also complete.

In its four corners
one should properly draw the four goddesses.
In front of the outer maṇḍala
one should properly draw the four goddesses:
Sattvī, Ratnī, likewise Dharmī,
and Karmī; in the wheel maṇḍala,
the graceful one, the garland, likewise song,
and the dancer, of the graceful ones.
The excellent great jewel family,
all pervaded by the jewel mudrās,
their supreme hands marked by their own mudrās,
encircled by garlands of light rays, radiant,
empowered by the jewel vajra,
each holding his own mudrā,
bearing various jewel weapons,
arisen from jewels, possessing jewels,
dwelling in a house whose nature is jewels,
with incense and jewel flowers,
dwelling upon great supreme jewels,
their bodies clothed in jewel garments.

This completes the section called the Great Jewel Family, from the great maṇḍala, the king of all maṇḍalas, called the Secret, the Supreme Secret.

Having gone with vajra speed
to the western lotus maṇḍala,
one should properly draw with great joy
the excellent great lotus family.
In its western maṇḍala
one should properly draw the five maṇḍalas.
In the wheel at the central hub
one should place the form of a buddha:
an excellent lotus of eight petals,
eight petals, lovely,
the one with the yoga of the stable dharmadhātu,
beautified by a garland of lotuses.
In its center, the great king,
having all forms, supreme in nature,
the Buddha endowed with wisdom and skillful means,
holding the wheel of Dharma.
Abiding on that single lotus,
resting upon its maṇḍala,
one should draw the four lotus beings:
the wheel-turning monarch, the fearless,
adorned with every ornament,
endowed with the empowerment of the buddha lotus;
one should draw Vajrapadma and the rest.
The lotus beings, fearless,
all wearing the crowns of buddhas,
empowered with the garland of Dharma,
bearing lotus emblems and weapons,
sporting in the magical display of the buddhas:
Padmasattva, and likewise the king,
Ratna, and Dharma of the great way;
the four supreme samayas
one should properly draw in due order.

In the eastern maṇḍala of the west,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center, the great king,
one should place the various wheel.
Well established in the essence of the Dharma,
resting upon the eight-petaled lotus,
in the center of the lotus anthers
one should place the one having all forms,
adorned with every ornament,
possessing a body of beautiful form,
endowed with the empowerment of the five buddhas,
three-eyed and eight-armed.
Brahmā is on the topknot, Indra on the forehead,
the flower-weaponed one between the brows;
on the eyes one should place the Sun and the Moon;
the Aśvins and Kumāra on the ears;
Viṣṇu is the tip of the nose;
the Black One at the fangs, and Big Belly;
on the tongue is Sarasvatī,
and on the voice, the Great Sarasvatī;
Kumuda and the one with the lotus garland
dwell at the mouth and the cheeks;
on the eyes, the Perfection of Wisdom;
half of the face sends forth emanations;
on the right shoulder is the lion,
and on the left the boar properly dwells;
in the two nostrils
are Sāgara and Upatapta;
Nanda and Upananda on the upper arms;
in the belly the Ocean dwells;
on the right thigh dwells Mahābala;
Gaṇapati on the upper arm;
the Great God (Śiva), Maheśvara,
dwells at the door above the sexual organ;
Śrīvatsa dwells at the heart,
and also the great goddess Śrī;
at the door of the lower opening
the king Yama rests;
Durgā and the great terrifying goddess
properly dwell on the knees;
on the ten toes
the ten mātṛkās dwell;
Rudra dwells at the nape;
the water goddess dwells on the back;
the gods of spring, early summer, summer,
autumn, winter and late winter,
on the right and the left,
properly dwell on the feet.
On the thousand-spoked wheel below,
the binding of Rudra's aggregates and

Rāhu, Vemacitrin and the Illuminator,
and all the asuras dwell all around.
On the palms of the ten hands
the ten perfections dwell.
Just as one wishes,
draw these, the Great God and the others.
With four faces and eight arms,
each face having three eyes,
endowed with fine garments and a fine form:
one should set out the Sage of such a nature.
In all of its directions
draw Padmasattva properly.
Padmasattva, the hook,
the arrow and Padma Sādhu are to be duly assigned.
In the supreme southern quarter of the west,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center, the great being,
draw Ratnapadma properly.
Having twelve faces,
the supreme one endowed with twelve arms,
properly seated on a jewel lotus,
is held to arise from the jewel.
In all of its directions
draw Padmaratna, the net and the others:
Bhṛkuṭī, the Sun, the Banner,
and likewise the Eleven-Faced One.
In the maṇḍala of the western direction,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center, the great being,
Amitābha is to be set out.
Bestowing the great lotus,
Padmanarteśvara, the chief,
endowed with a thousand eyes and arms,
holds all the mudrās and weapons;
noble, with twelve faces,
properly accompanied by the lotus goddess,
endowed with the ten perfections,
he sports in the midst of the hosts.
In all of its directions
draw Padmasattva properly:
Tārā, the mighty Kumāra,
and Nīlakaṇṭha, born from the color of Brahmā.
In the northern part of the west,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in its center, the great being,
Padma and the others are to be set out.
With six faces and twelve arms,
the supreme one holding mudrās and weapons,
the great one bestowing manifold things:
draw Padmakarma properly.
In all of its directions
is Padmanarteśvara, the king.
Dwelling in front of the maṇḍala,
draw the Padma Rākṣasa, the yakṣa,
and the Padma Fist properly.
Right and left, behind and
in front: this is the maṇḍala.

Then the heart is ha. The heart of the buddhas is hrīḥ; it is the heart of all. Here ends the fourth section from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala called the Secret and the Supreme Secret.

Having completed the maṇḍala
of the hosts of the lotus family,
at the four corners of its wheel
draw the four goddesses properly.
Padmasattvā, Ratnā,
Karmā and Dharmā, draw them properly;
Lāsyā the graceful, Mālā of the garland, likewise Gītā of song,
and this Nṛtyā of the dance, draw them properly.

Here ends the section called the Great Lotus Family from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala.

In the northern maṇḍala of karma,
having gone there with vajra speed,
for its king of maṇḍalas
set out five wheels, five by five.
In its center, the great karma,
set out the form of the Buddha,
the great king who holds the Dharma,
seated upon a garuḍa seat.
In all of its directions
draw the karma mudrās properly:
Karmasattva, Ratna,
likewise Karma and Dharmatā.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
in the eastern maṇḍala of the north,
Akṣobhya, the great buddha,
Vajrakarma is to be set out.
Its offerings, together with weapons,
are held together with its mudrās:
the noble holder of great karma,
properly endowed with the deeds of a buddha.
In all of its directions
draw Karmasattva properly:
the sattva, Rāga, the great king,
Sādhu, and likewise Karma itself.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
in the southern part of the north,
the great king of the jewel samādhi,
draw Ratnakarma properly.
He sends down a rain of jewels
and utterly destroys all suffering.
In all of its directions
draw Ratnakarma properly:
the splendor of the sattva, the Banner,
Karma, and Hāsa, the purifier.
The noble Karmaratna
is properly endowed with the jewel maṇḍala.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
in the western part of the north,
the great Padmakarmasattva,
draw him properly in the center of the maṇḍala.
In all of its directions
draw Padmakarma properly:
the Sharp Lotus, likewise the Cause,
and likewise Speech: this is karma.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
in the northern maṇḍala of the north,
the holder of great karma, the powerful one,
draw the king of karma properly.
In all of its directions
set out Karmasattva:
the Karma Rākṣasa, the yakṣa,
and likewise the Fist of Gathering.

Then the heart is aḥ. The heart of the buddhas is aḥ; it is the heart of all. Here ends the fifth section from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Wheel Maṇḍala called the Secret. The maṇḍala of the northern part is also complete.

Having drawn the maṇḍala in the north,
and having completed the karma maṇḍala,
at the four corners of its wheel
set out the four goddesses.
The four earlier samaya beings,
all together, are thirty-three.
The one called the center has thirty-three;
in the eastern direction there are thirty-three;
likewise the western quarter;
the north and the south are likewise.

Here ends the section called the Karma Family from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala, the Secret and the Supreme Secret. The entire retinue of the maṇḍala is also complete.

The hundred and sixty-fifth.

Then the maṇḍala, the supreme wheel,
the great city whose nature is liberation:
at the four corners of the great wheel
four maṇḍalas are to be drawn.
The great union of the wheel, in the southeast,
is to be set out in the form of the moon.
In the center of the moon maṇḍala
Vajrasattva is to be set out,
smiling, with the color of the moon,
blazing with manifold red rays of light,
properly seated in the cross-legged posture of the sattva,
the supreme one, holding the vajra in a proud manner,
in his right hand holding a vajra,
and likewise in his left holding a bell;
the great being in meditative equipoise,
who ponders the welfare of all sentient beings,
properly dwells upon his lotus.
Relying on that maṇḍala of light,
draw the four goddesses properly;
thus the five secret ones are properly shown.
The first and the second
properly dwell on the right side;
the third and the fourth
dwell on the left side.
The goddess who proclaims the vajra clamor,
his own goddess, embracing him with her knees,
turning her head at his side,
smiling and gazing all around,
clasps the hand of the Blessed One.
Raising the makara banner,
at his side, laughing,
is the striker, the Mistress of Beings.
She who proclaims the sound of the vajra clamor,
likewise Vajra Unfailing Joy,
the goddess Vajra Kāmeśvarī,
and the Vajra Lady of Splendid Joy.
In the southwestern corner,
having gone there with vajra speed,
in the center of its maṇḍala
a great lotus is to be set out.
In the center of the lotus maṇḍala
Padmasattva is to be set out,
with four faces and eight arms,
with a wheel and a maṇḍala of light;
he properly dwells upon his lotus.
Relying on that maṇḍala of light,
draw properly the four goddesses
born from the lotus family.
Of the wholly secret goddesses
the names shall next be explained:
Vajraśrī and Vajra White,
Vajra Lamp and the Vajra Bearer.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
in the maṇḍala of the northwestern corner,
having drawn a maṇḍala of the sky,
properly endowed with a vajra sun,
in the center of the sun maṇḍala
Ratnasattva is to be set out,
pervaded by blue and red light,
adorned with every ornament;
he properly dwells upon his lotus.
Relying on that maṇḍala of light,
draw properly the four goddesses
born from the great jewel family:
She Born from Space, Sky Vajra,
the Sky-Colored Mistress of Beings,
the great goddess Glorious Jewel,
and the Beauty of the Family, with her jewel.
Having gone there with vajra speed,
on the wheel of the northeastern corner,
likewise with the petals of a blue utpala,
the maṇḍala is to be drawn properly.
In the center of that blue utpala,
the supreme great holder of the karmas,
proclaimed the karma of all the buddhas and so forth:
Vajrakarma is to be set out.
He properly dwells upon his lotus.
Relying on that maṇḍala of light,
draw properly the four goddesses
born from the karma family:
Karmamudrā, Vajraratnā,
Karmā and Dharmā, the great glorious ones.
Just as earlier in the vajra family,
so in all the maṇḍalas
that very one dwells as the messenger,
that very one properly dwells as the goddess,
that very one is Lāsyā, Vilāsinī and the rest,
and she also forms a further ocean of mudrās.
Yellow, red, like the sun,
they become like the color of the blue utpala.
Proud, endowed with the peaceful empowerment,
each fully holds her own mudrā.

Then the heart is hūṃ hrīḥ oṃ aḥ. Here ends the sixth section from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala called the Secret and the Supreme Secret. The maṇḍalas of the corners are also complete.

Endowed with one hundred and eighty-five,
the wheel having been completed,
on the sides of its corners
draw the four deities properly.
In the east is the great vajra,
the vajra blazing like fire,
the emblem of the great vajra family,
most terrifying with its garlands of flame.
In the south, the perfect jewel:
Varuṇa, the jewels and the makaras,
the emblem of the great jewel family,
proclaimed the source of the Buddha's Dharma.
In the west the earth goddess is to be drawn,
of the one called the Mighty Person,
bearing the great maṇḍala;
she is also the emblem of the lotus family.
In the north, the person of the wind,
whose garments are borne by the force of the wind,
and likewise the holder of the karma family,
moving and mighty, is to be drawn.
At one hundred and eighty-nine
accomplish the central maṇḍala properly:
the great ship of the way of the Dharma,
the great city whose nature is liberation.

Here ends the Great Wheel Maṇḍala called the Secret, from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala.

The supreme holder of vajra knowledge,
of the common and supremely secret one:
the maṇḍala of the wheel of one's own family
I shall explain all in one place.
In the center of all the maṇḍalas
is Vairocana, Lord of Sages,
seated on a lion and lotus seat;
he holds supreme awakening.
Akṣobhya rides an elephant;
the Lord of Jewels rides a horse;
the Blessed One Amitāyus
is properly seated on a peacock throne;
the unsurpassed Amoghasiddhi
is properly seated on the lord of garuḍas.
These are declared to be their mounts.
This is the division of all the supreme families.

Akṣobhya's is the earth-pressing gesture;
likewise the Lord of Jewels grants the supreme boon;
Amitāyus's grants samādhi;
Amoghasiddhi's grants fearlessness.
Blue, yellow, and likewise red,
and the green color that holds karma;
the lovely family of the Tathāgata
is proclaimed white in the color of its body.
Likewise, for the precious vajra,
the vajra is marked with a vajra garland:
arising from the union of the vajra maṇḍala,
the vajra lines are to be drawn properly.
For the noble great lotus family,
the lotus is marked with a lotus garland:
arising from the union of the lotus maṇḍala,
the lines are to be laid with a lotus thread.
For the noble jewel family,
the jewel is marked with a jewel garland:
arising from the union of the jewel maṇḍala,
the jewel lines are utterly beautiful.
For the karma family the variegated is taught,
and likewise for the supreme buddha family;
yet it is distinguished by the vajra.
Of the one arising from the union of the vajra maṇḍala,
the vajra is in the eastern quarter,
in the south is the jewel maṇḍala,
in the west the lotus is properly taught,
and in the north karma is properly joined.
All the vajra bearers are frowning;
the host of the jewel is proclaimed;
the great lotus is tamed and peaceful;
the holders of karma are fierce and peaceful.
On the wheel of the great maṇḍala
the great wheel is to be drawn properly.
Then the outer wheel and the rest:
this whole rite is like that.

Here ends the seventh section, called the Secret, from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala of the Great Secret, the Supreme Secret. What arises from the yoga of karma is complete.

Having completed the inner maṇḍala,
one should fill it with colored powders.
All of the lines and the drawings
are to be filled entirely with every color.
From the first to the last,
draw the outer maṇḍala all around.
Even, with equal portions all around,
lay it out as a square,
with four corners and four doors,
beautified by four archways,
properly endowed with eight pillars,
pleasing and well divided,
properly endowed with four lines,
and adorned with silk and garlands.
With a thread of vajra jewels
lay the lines of the outer maṇḍala.
Surround it entirely with a rim,
beautified with a vajra rim,
pervaded by tendrils of half-moons,
endowed with clusters of vajra garlands,
decorated with nets and half-nets,
beautified by half-moons,
properly endowed with nets of bells,
and adorned with manifold colors.
On the line between that and the center
draw the bodhisattvas properly:
the thousand of the Fortunate Eon,
the great ones of miraculous power who have gone before.
In the east all are marked with vajras;
in the south they hold jewel emblems;
in the west lotus, vajra and the rest;
in the north they hold karma vajras.
Whatever gods there are of great miraculous power,
draw them properly on the outer line.
The great gods, Rudra and the others,
have half their bodies emerging from the band.
Maheśvara, the great Brahmā,
Viṣṇu and Sanatkumāra,
the kings with their families and hosts:
draw them properly in the eastern maṇḍala.
He of the Coiled Light, Caṇḍa,
Piṅgala and Vajrapiṅgala,
with their families, properly endowed with wheels:
draw them properly in the southern direction.
The Intoxicated One, Mālin, Jaya,
Vijaya and the Escorts,
with their families and properly endowed with hosts:
draw them properly in the western part.
The Silk-Clad One, Agni and Vāyu,
Vajrabhairava and the others,
below, from the western part,
draw them properly in the northern part.
Likewise, with their hosts and lords,
the mātṛkās, the World-Renowned One,
the chief who sports with the wheel,
the asuras and the others of great miraculous power,
are declared to be of the family of the outer wheel.
Those taught by the ten thousand
in the great tantra called the Secret,
draw as you wish.
At the corners of the outer maṇḍala
four maṇḍalas are to be drawn.
Endowed with flowers, incense and the rest,
draw the offering goddesses properly.
As you are able to draw,
as is beautiful and fitting,
as your own family is taught,
draw as you wish.

Here ends the eighth section, called the Supremely Beautiful Outer Maṇḍala, from the King of All Maṇḍalas, the Great Maṇḍala called the Secret and the Supreme Secret. The great secret maṇḍala of the tathāgatas is complete in its entirety.

Then, in this secret maṇḍala of all the tathāgatas, there arises this summoning of the entire awakening of the buddhas, namely: the summoning of the awakening mind and of the six perfections; the causing of great skill in means to enter the irreversible ground; the facing toward every aim; the abiding in nirvāṇa; the gathering of the accumulations for great awakening; the not deceiving with regard to the welfare of sentient beings; the not giving rise to a wish for any vehicle other than this Great Vehicle, and the binding to it unchangeably; and the full accomplishment of the great wisdom of the buddhas, which gathers means, wisdom, samādhi, dhāraṇī, conduct and the unsurpassed qualities of the buddhas.

There, at the eastern door, the Vajradhara called Great Vajra Hook is to be set out. In the south the one called Vajra Noose, in the west the one called Vajra Chain, and in the north the great Vajradhara called Vajra Bell are to be set out.

Here ends the great maṇḍala called Entry, from the special declaration of the way of the great bodhisattvas, from the Great Maṇḍala called the Secret, King of All Maṇḍalas. This is the wheel of the Tathāgata.

It brings attainment of the way of the bodhisattvas.
When the maṇḍala has been fully completed,
the being of the great supreme samaya
should circumambulate it three times.
The secret ones are to be inspected without omission,
and when all are assembled one should rejoice.
If a secret one is missing, it is to be made good,
and any excess is to be cleared away.
Then, as Vajrasattva,
one should quickly employ the great mudrā
and recite at one time
the sublime one hundred and eight names.
The vajra goddesses properly dwelling
at the corners of the wheel are to be invited.
The buddha stūpas dwelling
in the outer maṇḍala are to be invited.
The door guardians, who dwell
in the middle of the doors, are to be invited.
The great beings dwelling
in the outer maṇḍala are to be invited.
Then at all the doors,
performing the actions of the hook and the rest,
bind the four mudrās properly,
and do so one hundred and eight times.
With the vajra hook summon them all;
with the vajra noose draw them in;
with the vajra chain bind them all;
with the vajra bell make them descend.
This is the action at the doors.
Then, having taken one's seat in the center,
the vajra master, great in austerity,
with the mudrā of vajra opening
should open all the doors.
Visualizing oneself as the door guardian,
one is to open the doors.
With the mudrā of the vajra threatening finger
threaten all the malevolent ones.
When one properly begins the offering,
until its time is complete,
in all of the corners
the incense smoke is to be wafted.
First performing the action at the doors,
then make the offerings properly:
first flowers, trees and so on,
incense and lamps, together with their mudrās.
Before the time of the twilight juncture,
the vajra vase is to be invited.
With the mudrā of the vajra vase
invite it as best one can.
A single, very excellent vase,
beautiful, made of the seven precious substances,
or else one mixed with jewels,
is to be filled with vajra perfumed water.
With the mantra of vajra water
and the vajra mudrā, the pure water,
when one has recited one thousand and eight times,
becomes the water of vajra nectar.

The mantra of the vase is oṃ vajra udaka hūṃ. The mantra of the flowers is oṃ vajra puṣpe hūṃ. The mantra of the lamps is oṃ vajra āloke hūṃ. The mantra of the perfume is oṃ vajra gandā hūṃ. The mantra of the incense is oṃ vajra dhūpe hūṃ.

Incense, flowers and the rest,
all are to be joined with their mudrās.
Having taken up the vajra mudrā,
apply them with the vajra mantras.
Beginning at the time of evening,
perform the rite of bathing properly.
Anoint the body with perfume
and adorn it well with every ornament.
By the mantra of vajra speed
and by the mudrā of vajra speed,
in going out and in entering,
the vajra yoga is not faulted.
Even if one should step upon
the eyebrows, the vajra line drawings and emblems,
the host of buddha mudrās and the rest,
or any of the beings,
the vajra yoga is not faulted,
and there will be no regret for it.
By ringing the vajra bell,
even if the rite is deficient or in excess,
it will be accomplished and not be faulted.
If one is equipped with that rite,
there is nothing at all to fear.
Because of the armor of accomplished yoga,
the buddhas do not hold it as a fault.
With the great mudrā of Vajrasattva
one should enter to worship the maṇḍala.
Having entered, in the center of the maṇḍala
the vajras are to be summoned.
Having bound the vajra hook completely,
one summons them with the finger.
That very vajra hook
is declared in the tantra to be the summoning.
Likewise, for the great vajra family,
bind the vajra hook properly.
By the application of the jewel hook
the jewels are to be summoned.
The mudrā of the lotus hook
is taught for the great lotus family.
With the mudrā of the karma hook
summon the great karma family.
Binding the hook of the great vajra,
invite all the buddhas.
With the mudrā of the maṇḍala hook
invite all the maṇḍalas.
Binding the outer vajra hook,
invite the outer vajra family.
With the hook of the auspicious vajra
invite the bodhisattvas.
By the application of the hook of supreme awakening
invite those of the maṇḍala.
With the mudrā of the jewel hook
invite the vajra holders.
With the mudrā of the heart hook
invite Vajrapāṇi.
By the application of the vajra hook
recite twenty-one times for all.
With the mantra mudrā of the proud manner
invite Vairocana properly.
By the application of the buddha hook,
likewise, the buddhas themselves.
With the mudrā of the samaya hook
invite the samaya beings.
Having performed the application of summoning,
recite the one hundred and eight names.
When the hundred and eight names are complete,
produce the clouds of offerings properly.
Practice the discipline as it has been taught,
the offering of oneself and the rest:
confession, supplication and offering,
the offering of oneself and the rest.
When one's own duties are complete,
properly begin the binding of the mudrās.
By a hundred, or else a thousand,
or a gathering of a hundred hundreds,
having bound the host of mudrās,
the maṇḍalas will be accomplished.
Then, having performed the maṇḍala rite,
for as long as it takes for dawn to break,
for that long bind the mudrās properly:
this surely bestows accomplishments.
Then rising, together with the mudrās,
having come outside the maṇḍala,
threatening all the malevolent ones and the rest,
one should enter to worship the maṇḍala.
Having bathed oneself, the body anointed with perfume,
with ornaments and clothing as befits,
wearing exceedingly beautiful garments,
garlanded, with a sweet-smelling mouth,
by the application of the vajra dance
one places a flower on the crown of the head,
and with the joy of the sporting vajra
enters and bows low to the Protector.
With that very passion and attachment,
and with that very mudrā of urging,
all the buddhas, who bestow
all accomplishments, are to be urged:
"Free of faults and permanent,
attached with passion to all,
great one worthy of worship, great king,
Blessed One, accomplish that very thing for me!"
Then, having bound the mudrā
of the supreme samaya according to the rite,
the vajra master, having entered,
binds the chain mudrā properly.
Then the vajra master
binds that very vajra hook,
and by snapping the fingers above
all the buddhas are to be gathered.

The mantra for gathering the buddhas is jaḥ jaḥ jaḥ. At the eastern door, the syllable jaḥ.

Likewise in the south, the syllable hūṃ;
in the west, of the great chain, vaṃ;
in the north, of the causing to descend, aḥ.
For all the vajra maṇḍalas
these actions are properly taught.
When the actions have been completely performed,
then wonders will be seen:
great miraculous displays, great marvels,
hundreds of kinds and tenfold:
these great marvelous miraculous displays
will be seen by the vajra master.
All the great beings of accomplishment
will come to every maṇḍala.
The vajra bearers and the bodhisattvas,
likewise the five families,
the Vajradhātu, the great being,
and the supreme Blessed One will also come.
Vajrasattva and the other children of the buddhas:
the master will see these wonders.
The offerings and the tormas
will be complete with the rite as it should be.
Likewise, having seen the miraculous powers and accomplishments,
one is to carry out the maṇḍala.
According to how the wheel is arranged,
according to how the requisites are expended,
and according to how the discipline is practiced,
so will the accomplishments be attained.
The bodhisattvas of great power
and the buddhas will truly be seen.
That the great wheel comes in such a way:
this is the supreme wonder.
Just as they are seated,
so one takes hold of the wheel completely,
and one attains the sublime marvel
pervaded by excellent wisdom.
All the marvels of the maṇḍala
will be seen, bursting forth from the earth;
or else the maṇḍala will be seen
abiding in the sky, in the midst of space;
or else an ocean of buddhas,
filled with all the buddhas, will be seen;
or else in all the directions
the bodies of the buddhas will be clearly seen.
In the form of Vajrasattva
the vajra bearer sees himself;
or else as the one called the Five Secret Ones;
or else as the buddha, supreme among the wise;
or else that place of the maṇḍala
encompasses the three thousand worlds;
or else Vajrasattva alone
is declared the lord of the maṇḍala.
Its night is seen as day,
pervaded entirely by the light of the sun;
or else it is the rising sun,
or else the disc of the moon.
Having seen a pure realm,
one will see the play of the buddhas.
One will see the empowerment
of the utterly pure assembly.
These wondrous displays, called
the manifold marvels, will be seen.
For the vajra master of great fortune,
the worship of the maṇḍala becomes manifold.
But even when the requisites are gathered,
whoever is obscured
will not see such wonders.
Then properly begin another yoga:
having bound all the mudrās,
properly perform the tearing apart of wrongdoing.
These are the manifold marvels:
for some, merely by entering,
accomplishment is beyond doubt;
accomplishment comes in the first watch.
Likewise for some, at midnight;
for a third, at the break of dawn, accomplishment comes;
likewise for some, when the sun has risen,
immediately the desired
supreme awakening of a buddha is accomplished.
If the fortunate one should desire liberation,
the first ground is what one desires:
having attained the ground of Utter Joy,
one goes by it to the non-abiding.
Or else one becomes a vidyādhara,
or likewise becomes the vajra itself.
One accomplishes just as one wishes,
and fully takes hold of the awakening of the buddhas.
Even one who commits the five acts of immediate retribution,
and likewise commits every wrongdoing,
when he has done it seven times,
will accomplish the yoga by practice.
He is no longer embodied from the four elements;
he will forever have the form of space;
he will become the dharmadhātu itself.
The vajra command is not otherwise.
For those who practice seven times,
wrongdoing becomes virtue itself,
and misdeeds become a mass of merit.
Otherwise there will be no accomplishment:
when one remains without faith,
and when one gives way to doubt,
that yoga will degenerate.
If the awakening mind is not fully embraced,
the prior approach practice will degenerate,
and the discipline will always degenerate.
Such and other obstacles are many;
these forms of pride are to be utterly given up.
One who has not fully taken the vows
does not know the way of that yoga;
the vajra master will be unstable,
and there will be no accomplishment.
Therefore, with every effort,
if one is to become a vajra master,
learned and having taken the vows,
the awakening mind is to be fully embraced.
When one's life is to be meaningful,
and if one wants a birth that bears fruit,
then with every effort
properly begin the great yoga.
Having come to possess the awakening mind,
holding the vows of the knowledge mantra,
then properly begin the yoga:
the awakening of the buddhas will be accomplished.

Again it was asked:
"In order to accomplish the tantra of the knowledge mantras,
how should the vows be taken?
How should the samaya be guarded?
What is a degeneration of the samaya?"

For the sake of the welfare of all sentient beings,
having properly generated the awakening mind,
one should not wish for another vehicle;
then properly begin the yoga.
Whatever maṇḍala one may be in,
go before the master,
bow at his feet and say:
"Great Joy, you are my teacher;
master, please consider me.
Great Protector, you who show the way of the bodhisattvas,
I request of you:

Please grant me that very samaya;
grant me the vows as well.
Lord, grant me the vows."

The master should examine him closely
and question him in every way.
Whoever is a fit vessel
for the samaya to be declared, him he should admit.
Not because one is a son or a disciple:
it should be given to one who is a vessel.
