I myself am the king;
listen to my command!"
Hearing the words of the Great God,
Vajra Wrath, blazing fiercely,
proclaimed the king of wrath
called Vajrayakṣa the Crusher:
oṃ vajra ni sumbha hūṃ
By the uttering of this wrathful one,
exceedingly ferocious, of great strength,
Maheśvara fainted
and likewise fell to the ground.
Motionless, he came under the power of death;
he lay there like a felled tree,
without stirring, without moving,
tormented by Vajra Wrath.
The Blessed One said:
"Vajra Wrath, all-knowing one,
why is this great being
exhausting the cause of his life
and bringing him to ruin?"
Then King Vajradhara,
urged on by the Buddha's compassion,
spoke this vajra vidyā
called Vajra Life.

oṃ vajra āyuḥ
Through the uttering of this vidyā
Maheśvara rose up.
Restored to life by Vajra Life,
he attained great bliss.
Bowing down at the feet
of Vairocana, the great sage, he made this plea:
"Protector, Blessed One, be my refuge!
Is that Vajradhara not cruel?"
The all-knowing Blessed One said:
"I am not able to show you mercy.
This one is exceedingly fierce toward the malevolent;
go for refuge to him himself."
Vajra Wrath summoned the Great God
and spoke these words:
"Great malevolent one, listen
to the commands of my teaching!"
To Vajrayakṣa, the great being,
the Great God replied:
"I am able to bear a great death,
but I will not abide by your command."
When, through anger, the malevolence grew,
the greatly fierce Vajra Wrath
uttered the fierce heart mantra,
the exceedingly terrifying hūṃ:
hūṃ
Through the uttering of the syllable hūṃ
Maheśvara fell once again.
The greatly fierce Vajra Wrath
recited the drawing in by the feet:
oṃ pāta ākarṣaṇa vajra hūṃ
Then this Vajra Lord,
by reciting, brought forth Vajra Wrath,
and these words were spoken.
Proclaiming the command, with great light,
Vajra Wrath commanded:
oṃ bāta ākarṣaya sarva vajradhara kacara phaṭ vajra hūṃ jaḥ
When the great wrathful one had spoken this,
Maheśvara together with Umā
was drawn in by the vajra feet,
by the vajra that draws in by the feet.
Vajra Wrath drew them in,
feet up and heads down;
the whole world was set laughing,
and they were tormented by vajra torment.
Then King Vajradhara,
pressing down under his feet
Maheśvara together with Umā,
struck Maheśvara with the vajra.
With his left foot
he pressed down Maheśvara,
and with his right he squeezed the goddess Umā
between her breasts.
With this secret vajra mantra
he pressed Maheśvara down hard:
oṃ vajra ākrama ho
Once again Vajra Wrath
recited the vidyā of possession:
oṃ vajra āveśa hana hana traṭa
Thereupon Maheśvara and those with him,
with their own hands, a thousand times,
struck their own faces terrifyingly;
it came to a full thousand blows.
All who dwell in the three worlds
cried out with a great wail:
"This king of ours
has come to such a state!"
Then in King Vajradhara
love and compassion arose.
Placing compassion foremost,
he recited the Buddha's Loving-kindness:
oṃ buddha maitri rakṣa
When the glorious lord Vajradhara
then spoke this mantra,
the suffering of Maheśvara
was stilled in that very instant.
Through being touched by the feet of Vajrapāṇi,
Maheśvara attained
the great ground,
and likewise samādhi and dhāraṇī.
Casting off his ordinary body,
he offered it to Vajradhara.
The Great God died and passed on from here,
and having died and cast off his body,
beyond buddha fields equal in number
to the sands of thirty-two Ganges rivers,
there is a delightful buddha field
called Bhasmacchanna, Covered with Ash.
Going there, the Great God
displayed the arising of a buddha:
there he abides as the Tathāgata
Bhasmeśvaranirghoṣa, Lord of Ash with Melodious Voice.
Then that dead body
proclaimed a solemn utterance;
through the power of all the buddhas
it spoke forth a single verse:
"How wonderful, the unsurpassed
buddha wisdom of all the buddhas!
Having been slain, in an instant
I have been set in the state of nirvāṇa."
The great king Vajra Wrath
brought forth Maheśvara,
and summoning Viṣṇu
and the other gods, he said:
"Lords, enter fully
into this most supreme maṇḍala!
If you do not do so,
you will be made like Maheśvara."
Then all the gods were afraid,
and terrified, they all said:
"We wish to do just that.
We have become your servants.
King, how could we not
accomplish your samaya?
We are your retinue;
give us your command for a great eon!"
Vajra Wrath said:
"Go for refuge
to the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha.
Having truly generated the mind of awakening,
devote yourselves wholly to all sentient beings.
Give up the taking of life;
stealing and speaking falsehood,
and likewise the wives of others:
give these up first of all.
Whatever brings harm to sentient beings
you should by no means do.

Applying yourselves fully to the conduct of awakening, be diligent in it and never slack. These are the samaya of the command of my teaching, rightly declared. Listen to my words! Otherwise I shall burn you completely." Then the circle of the three worlds, having accepted those words, truly generated the mind of awakening and went for refuge to the Three Jewels. The great king Vajra Wrath, having conferred the mudrā upon all, by means of this heart of the mudrā had them enter the maṇḍala of Vajra Tirinṭiri: oṃ vajra samaya gṛhṇa buddha samaye vajrasatva samaya manusmara sarva tathāgata samaya traṃ dṛḍho me bhava apratiharo me bhava sarva karma su ca me cittaṃ śreyaḥ kuru ha ha ha ha hūṃ Then by its mere recitation the mudrā descended upon all, and all the mudrās of Vajra Tirinṭiri were shown. Having had them enter in due order, he empowered them with the vajra jewel. Placing the vajra emblem in their hands, he empowered them with vajra names. There all the gods received the empowerment of vajra names. First the lords of the three worlds were empowered in due sequence. The one who abides as Viṣṇu became known as Illusion Vajra. Kumāra became Vajra Bell, Brahmā the Vajra Sage, Indra the Vajra Weapon: these are declared to be lords of vidyā. The four kings of great splendor, of great miraculous power and great strength, who move through the sky, were empowered with vajra names: the Sun became Vajra Coil, likewise the Moon Vajra Light, the Great Staff Vajra Staff, and Piṅgala Vajra Piṅgala. These and other kings of wrath were empowered as vajra wrathful ones. Then the gods who move in the sky were empowered with vajra names: the Great Intoxicated became the Vajra Intoxicated, Vajra Garland the Honey Maker; Vijaya and Jayakara, Vajra Knowledge and the Victorious, the kings, the Gaṇapatis: these and others were empowered. Then those who dwell upon the earth were empowered with vajra names: the Guardian of the Treasury became the Pestle, Fire likewise became Vajra Fire, the Great Wind became Vajra Wind, and the Terrifier, Vajra Terrifier. Such messengers and others were empowered by the supreme vajra. Then those who dwell beneath the earth: Vajra Hook, the Leg Eater; likewise Yama became Vajra Black; the Crown of the Earth, the Obstructor Vajra; and Varuṇa, the Protector, Vajradhara. Then the goddesses, as was fitting, were empowered in due sequence: with the empowerment of vajra names the supreme mothers were empowered. Vaiṣṇavī became Vajra Gold, the Six-Faced One's consort Vajra Maiden, Brahmāṇī the Vajra Eightyfold Lady, Aindrī the Vajra Fist Lady, the Slayer the Vajra Slayer herself, Jyeṣṭhā the Vajra Radiant Lady, and then the Staff, the Supreme Vajra Lady. Hārītī became the Vajra Girdle, the Slayer the Vajra Terrifying Lady, Thunderbolt the Vajra Thunderbolt Lady, Revatī the Vajra Powerful Lady, the Clothed One the Vajra Clothed Lady, the Vajra Messenger the Peaceful Lady, Vāyu's goddess the Vajra Swift Lady, Kauberī the Vajra Raised Hood, the goddess of Fire likewise Vajra Blaze, Vārāhī the Vajra Faced Lady, Vajra Black Lady, Cāmuṇḍī, Varuṇa's goddess the Vajra Makara Lady, and the Noseless One the Emanation Lady. These and other goddesses without number were empowered. Each having realized her own family, they were declared, as was fitting, to be ladies of the deities. Then he gave the binding of the mudrā and likewise the heart of the samaya. All the lords of all the gods, having been summoned, likewise came under his power. Binding the two vajra mudrās, binding the threatening forefinger and the hook, for binding and for releasing, the being whose aim is the work to be done: before him you are to remain in order to accomplish his tasks. Lest your life be ruined, likewise do not give the samaya away. In full, in the great mudrā, through the Compendium of the Great Vehicle, the one who applies vajra yoga: this is what is taught as Reality. All those supreme maṇḍalas, and likewise all the gods and the rest, were summoned and made to enter fully. The Vajra Bearer, having taken control, empowered all of them with the vajra and marked them with vajra names. The Great God alone was struck down beneath his foot and kept there. He who had grown so malevolent in the world became known as the Heart of Malevolence. Into the maṇḍala of the king of malevolence beings will come, without any doubt. For a moment, on each day of the month, for a month, in that maṇḍala, hundreds of thousands of beings are made to suffer in that instant. There are those that confront the mind, diseases that steal away vitality, those that kill human beings, and even great poisons; those that destroy cities, towns and villages, those that cut off sentient beings, those that leave the land empty of people; those so fierce and terrifying, merciless, doing evil, killing and destroying, stealing away wisdom, taking various fierce forms, down even to the animal realm, harming sentient beings, swiftly stealing vitality; bad harvests, hail and rain, causing the rain not to fall, the torments of cold and heat produced in an instant, causing grahas to descend, surely giving rise to the supreme māras, distracting the mind: such yakṣas without end. Then King Vajradhara, lord of the abode of the Jewels, with the eye of wisdom beheld the people struck by various diseases. Beings hostile to the samaya, pestilence and likewise afflictions, fevers, grahas and fierce poisons are destroying these people. Stirred up by every kind of suffering, pervaded by various terrors, all ruined and overwhelmed, they suffer with fierce suffering. In order to make them happy once again, he took up fully the black mantra. Ḍākinīs, mātṛkās and yoginīs pervade all the affairs of beings; fevers, grahas and bhūtas, ḍākinīs, mātṛkās and bhūtas, and the malevolent pervade everywhere this world, this vessel of suffering.

Seeing this, King Vajradhara gave rise to compassion: "Alas, these beings suffer, and there is no one to show them mercy. I alone am self-arisen, and likewise I take away all suffering. They conceive of me wrongly, and for those people there is no mercy. Whoever is Maheśvara, and all the malevolent, I shall thoroughly tame. Perfect awakening will arise of itself; all people without exception are to be protected. All of them are to be summoned, and likewise the samaya is to be guarded. By doing both harm and benefit, these people will be liberated." Then King Vajradhara, the great being, entered into equipoise in the samādhi of the kind called Protecting and Benefiting the Maṇḍala. From the great protectors, all the buddhas, he received the command in this way. Then the heart mantra of all the buddhas was likewise brought forth: oṃ vajri sumbha ni sumbha hūṃ phaṭ Then King Vajradhara, taking up the Buddha's command, in order to summon the outer maṇḍala, brought forth the heart mantra of the hook: oṃ vajra samaya ākarṣaya sarva maṇdala vajradhara satva māti krama hūṃ phaṭ Through the recitation of the vidyā, from the Vajra Hook, utterly firm, supreme hooks of great being came forth in countless hundreds. This world of a billion worlds gradually dissolved completely. Whatever malevolent maṇḍalas were seen in the ten directions, having summoned those malevolent ones, he likewise made them into a single mass. Vajra Fire spoke out everywhere, and bringing down the summit of Meru, those beyond measure and beyond counting came into the outer maṇḍala. Throughout the abode of the Vajra Lord they took their places as the retinue. Then King Vajradhara called out to the malevolent maṇḍala: "O malevolent ones, give up harming people! Take up the samaya, and give up killing any living being! Otherwise, with my blazing right hand I shall likewise turn you to ash." The samaya maṇḍala of the malevolent made this request with a single voice: "Protector, we are piśācas; tell us what we are to do." Then the fierce Vajradhara, hearing the words of the unrestrained, with the command of Vajra Wrath, of great majesty, spoke the fierce heart mantra: oṃ vajra krodha hana daha paca vidhāṃsaya vikṛha sarva duṣṭān samaya mudrā manḍala bhañja bhañja mardha mardha khāda khāda pāra mantra vajra samaya hā phaṭ When the great wrathful one recited this, those exceedingly fierce malevolent ones were scorched and fell flat, and their samaya mudrā was broken. They wailed and wept, and spoke to one another: "Let us do just as he says; yet we need not speak the truth." Vajrasattva, the greatly fierce, by his very nature the self of the buddhas, the mighty Lord of Secrets, the all-knowing Blessed One, spoke: oṃ ni sumbha hana daha paca gṛhṇa bandha hūṃ phaṭ Then King Vajradhara, abiding fully in the Buddha's command, Vajra Wrath, the great devourer, recited the fierce heart mantra: oṃ mahā vajra krodha pāda sarva duṣṭa manḍala phinā śaya sarva duṣṭa samyayan vikira vidhvaṃsaya sphoṭaya bhañjaya bhañjaya sarva duṣṭa samaya mudrāṃ bhandha gṛhṇa ha nad ha paca sarva duṣṭa samaya satva samaya hūṃ phaṭ Then by its mere recitation, Vajra Wrath, the great being, ferocious, fierce, utterly terrifying, Vajra Wrath was brought forth. Seizing those hostile to the samaya, together with their families and their armies, he terrified them with utter terror, and even the ocean shook. By those terrifying mudrās they were destroyed and overpowered; burned by vajra fire, they were all turned to ash. How can the Vajra Bearer, arisen from the mind of awakening, burn sentient beings? These are words to be interpreted: born of the beneficial family, the lord of victors, knowing the stains of the afflictions, attachment, faults and views and the rest, the Vajra Bearer seized these stains and burned them with the fire of wisdom. Beings, greatly afraid and terrified, through the conduct of awakening abide in awakening; they are born in the abode of Akṣobhya, and the Vajra Bearer became supreme. Vajrapāṇi, the great being, entered fearlessly into equipoise in the samādhi of the kind called The Great Vajra Summoning the Mudrā. In order to train the ḍākinīs, he received the command from the Victor. Then all the buddhas spoke to the mighty Vajrapāṇi: oṃ hana hana vajra hūṃ phaṭ Then the fierce Vajradhara, having heard the words of all the buddhas, recited this mantra of his own, the vajra that summons the terrifying: oṃ vajra ākarṣaya śīghraṃ sarva duṣṭa gṛhṇa vajra satyena hūṃ jaḥ Then by its mere recitation, the ḍākinīs who steal vitality, together with their yoga, mantras and mudrās, all gathered at Meru. Summoned by the Vajra Hook, they blazed, wreathed in flames. Joining their palms at the crown of the head, they said: "Vajradhara, protect us!" The greatly fortunate Vajra Bearer said: "O great host of the malevolent, quickly give up killing and heed my command!" Each of them said: "We here are piśācas. Please teach us a means by which we may stay alive always." Then King Vajradhara spoke this means to them.

"Whoever has died once a month has passed, take that one as your food. Those who, free of attachment to the mind of awakening, make a practice of reviling the secret, I join together with you: I grant that you may devour them. This heart mantra of the mudrā you must always hold, and, abandoning all outsiders, guard the teaching of the Buddha. oṃ vajra pratigṛhṇa hṛdaya ākarṣaya caryayaṃ me sadhodhena miniyate hāsaridhayaṃ niṣkamadu samaya hūṃ jaḥ" Then King Vajradhara, endowed with his own mantra and mudrā, emanated the wrathful one, the King of the Crown, the yogin. "If you transgress the pledge, the great multicolored being will always split your heads and surely burn you together with your kin." In the samādhi called King of Nectar Medicine the great being became absorbed, and searched for the sake of curing disease. The Blessed One spoke: oṃ vajra hūṃ phaṭ Then King Vajradhara, summoning all the diseases, which destroy all sentient beings, spoke the heart mantra of summoning: oṃ vajra samaya ānaya sarva duṣṭa jvāraṇi dināśaya hūṃ phaṭ Then by its mere recitation, all the hosts of diseases and grahas, and the fevers and the rest, hearing it, feet upward and heads downward, wreathed in blazing fire, cried out "Ha ha!" in terror: "Without destroying our race, O Great Wrathful One, give us your command!" The great Vajra Yogin said: "Listen well to my command! Give up harming sentient beings, and do not steal their luster either!" They said: "O Vajra King, we beg you, protect our lives! We all feed on vital splendor; please devise some means for us." Then Vajrasattva, the great king, the one of great power, said: "Whoever upholds my mantras and mudrās, do them no harm. oṃ vajra karma viśodhaya sarva āparaṇani buddha satve samye hūṃ Moreover, all the great mudrās gathered in the Great Vehicle, all that is fully taught in the king of secret sūtra collections: in whatever place their mantras and mudrās are constantly practiced, in that place too, at any time, you must do no harm at all." When he had said this, all the grahas cried out "Hi hi!" and to the yogin of the crown they surrendered all their harm-doing. Then King Vajradhara, lord of the supreme abode of all, the one of great intelligence, became absorbed in the great mudrā free of evil. In order to bind the lower realms, he received the command from the protectors, and all the buddhas, the great protectors, together spoke the heart mantra: oṃ kṣepaya vajra svāhā The great means of Vajrasattva arose from the vajra. Out of love for sentient beings, all evils were summoned. oṃ sarva pāpaṃ apāyana viśodhana vajra samaya hūṃ phaṭ Having said this, the great yogin in an instant summoned the lower realms, and near the king of mountains wailing and the like were seen. Vajrasattva, the great yogin, said to the beings of the lower realms: "To the Three Jewels and to my teaching, quickly go for refuge, to the teaching!" All of them said: "O Mighty One, please protect us! Whatever the Lord has commanded, just so shall we do." Then King Vajradhara spoke the supreme secret that tears evil apart and surely binds, that utterly destroys all evil: oṃ vajrapāṇi visphoṭaya sarva apāya bhandhanāni pramokṣaya sarvāpāyagatabhyaḥ sarva satvaṃ sarva tathāgata vajra samya ṭvātha By this vidyā empowering the beings who bear the causes of the lower realms, the wheel of the lower realms was torn apart and fell headlong into the ocean. Likewise, dying and passing from the lower realms, all of them were fully liberated. Fully liberated from all suffering, they attained the state of the Vajra Lord. Likewise, at the feet of the Sage Vairocana they were born. By the power of Vajrasattva, the unfavorable states were likewise exhausted. By the power of Vajrasattva, in that very instant they attained peace. Various marvelous, greatly secret miracles came forth. This world of the trichiliocosm abided in the nature of vajra jewels, adorned with the seven precious substances, made beautiful with vajra jewels. All who had entered into its family were purified of every suffering. Beings came to be happy, fully endowed with vajra bliss. This field of the great supreme one became renowned as a pure field, pervaded everywhere by pure beings, likewise of the nature of the vajra yoga. Moreover, forests of trees and the like no longer appeared; in great marvel, all things of the nature of the seven precious substances grew forth from the forests and grasses. Free of all highs and lows, it appeared level like the palm of a hand, pleasant to the touch like kācali cloth: so the trichiliocosm came to stand. All the sufferings of the hells and the rest, the lower realms first of all, were stilled. The sufferings of hunger and thirst ceased entirely, and all abided in sameness. The oceans all dissolved away, and the forms of the mountains, too, spread out everywhere. Humans became equal to the gods of Paranirmitavaśavartin. Moreover, there was no encircling ring of mountains, no Meru, no Mandara, no abodes of the god realms, no ocean, and no asuras, nor any dwelling places of the nāgas. There were no human homes or houses; it was free of cities, regions and provinces, together with their caves. There were no human dwellings at all, no rivers, caves or valleys, nor even human bodies. No sentient being whatever appeared here. There was no wheel of the lower realms, no unfavorable states and no suffering, no women, men and the like, no camels, elephants or sheep. Wish-fulfilling trees and fragrant trees were beautiful, of the nature of the seven precious substances, and oceans of perfumed water were beautiful with many kinds of jewels. From all the delightful trees sounds of music poured forth, and the sounds of the Dharma in that music arose throughout the trichiliocosm. Just as the summit of Meru, the king of mountains, belonging to the Vajra Lord, so the abode of the trichiliocosm

blazed with light of various kinds.
This world of the trichiliocosm
came to stand perfectly square,
and all the outer abodes on every side
appeared opened out as well.
Again there was no sun and no moon,
there were no stars and no gods;
even within their own houses
the gods themselves could not be seen.
It was lit throughout by the light of the buddhas,
by captivating rays of many kinds,
through which the field was wholly purified,
delightful with vajra jewels.
Even the abode of the Vajra Lord
no longer appeared.
Elsewhere, in the abode of a single ground,
where the buddhas display their magical emanations,
this world of the trichiliocosm
was bright with divine lotuses,
beautiful with the seven precious substances,
filled with delightful masses of fragrance.
In the center of the field of the great eon,
upon a most lovely maṇḍala,
a vajra lion throne
eighty thousand yojanas in size arose.
All around, in all the directions,
each measuring sixty thousand yojanas,
beautiful, of the nature of vajra jewels,
four lion thrones arose.
Vajra canopies were spread out,
tassels of silk hung down,
nets of bells hung down,
and the field was adorned on every side.
Flowers of every kind were strewn all about;
it was covered with clouds of many kinds,
pervaded everywhere by many fragrances,
and adorned with wish-fulfilling trees.
The delightful wish-fulfilling trees
and beautiful vajra music
gave forth their sounds.
Together with this very field,
in a nature free of obstruction,
everything appeared in all directions.
Again there was nothing to fear.
This world of the trichiliocosm
was lit throughout by all the buddhas,
countless as the sands of the Ganges,
as many as the subtlest particles.
Like a pod filled with sesame seeds,
it was lit throughout by all the buddhas.
From every direction, without exception,
all the buddhas came here.
On the central lion seat,
in the fearless dharmadhātu,
Vairocana of great power,
the Teacher, the Blessed One, was seated.
In the east is Akṣobhya,
in the south is Ratnasambhava,
in the west is Amitābha,
in the north is Amoghasiddhi:
the great protectors in the four directions
are seated upon vajras.
In the directions and the intermediate directions
they abide, focused in sameness.
They were shown forth from the dharmadhātu,
and all the buddhas were firmly seated.
The five aspects of manifest awakening
made the dharmadhātu firm.
All the countless buddhas,
the Vajra Bearers, were seated upon the five.
Pervading each of the directions,
a single buddha was seated.
The intermediate directions, above and below,
Vairocana made firm.
The dharmadhātu having been made firm,
the buddha of the central direction was seated.
Then the magical emanations of the buddhas,
the essence of Dharma and complete enjoyment,
the magical emanations of the bodhisattvas,
all the buddhas, upon their own
five lion thrones were seated.
Countless and immeasurable,
the dharmatā of the buddhas without exception,
like a clear drawing,
became the entire trichiliocosm.
The bodhisattvas, in great joy,
were seated upon lotuses.
Possessing the central wheel,
five countless koṭis,
endowed with the play of the buddhas,
all the Vajra Bearers were seated.
Firmly seated in the eastern quarter,
five countless koṭis,
displaying vajra emanations,
were seated with their wheel.
All abiding in the southern direction,
five countless koṭis,
endowed with jewel emanations,
were seated with their wheel.
Five countless koṭis
abiding in the western direction,
displaying lotus emanations,
were seated with their wheel.
Five countless koṭis
abiding in the northern direction,
displaying action emanations,
were seated with their wheel.
Those of the secret and of offering,
the goddesses and the vajra goddesses,
countless and immeasurable:
the twelve filled the maṇḍala.
The gatekeepers, countless,
the four endowed with supreme yoga,
the kings, abide in the east and the south,
in the west and the north.
Arisen from the Dharma Body,
having become the Body of Complete Enjoyment,
all these great beings
arose from Dharma and complete enjoyment.
Whatever great-souled bodhisattvas
of the ten directions as well
had attained the ground of great yoga,
all of them, too, came here.
The hosts that entirely filled
as many buddha fields
as equal the sands of the Ganges,
all of them came.
Through the power of all the buddhas
the fields became vast,
and in all the lands, what was done
brought no harm and no injury.
Just as the world of the trichiliocosm
was before without any gap,
so the buddhas and bodhisattvas
abided without any gap.
There was not even a speck of space
in which the buddhas did not appear.
The bodhisattvas, the great yogins,
those of the forty grounds,
or those born of the Dharma,
the greatly renowned children of the Buddha,
those arisen from the five families and the rest,
those displaying the buddhas' magical emanations,
and the hosts of bodhisattvas dwelling here:
the supreme ones were seen in the directions and intermediate directions.
Of all the countless buddhas,
the gods dwelling in this place
saw only the five buddhas.
Apart from the buddhas, the arhats,
they saw no one at all in this field:
not the wheel of the vajra family,
nor the hosts of the maṇḍala.
Seated in the center and the directions,
the five buddhas were what they saw.
They saw Vajra Wrath,
who had harmed Maheśvara.
Together with the five buddhas, likewise,
six or seven wrathful ones abide.
Brahmā and the other gods
were struck with amazement.
Frightened and terrified, those gods
spoke to one another:
"We see the Lord just as he is:
what utterly wondrous thing is this that appears?
Again, the worlds of the gods
are not seen within existence;
there are no beings of the five destinies,
no Meru and no ocean;
the moon and the sun are not seen,
nor are there mountains or houses.
Have we become deluded,
or have our eyes gone blind,
or are our minds distracted?
What is this magical display of illusion?
We saw terror before:
fiercely summoned, we were reduced to ashes
on the summit of Meru, king of mountains,

we were tormented by great suffering.
Again, there is no Meru,
there is no Wrathful One of great miraculous power,
nor that other, the one Great Wrathful One
who brings harm to Maheśvara.
Even the place that had become small
appears here as vast.
This trichiliocosm, too, does not appear:
we have gone to another world."
Saying such various things,
they spoke to one another.
Then, falling silent,
they gazed at the Vajra Bearer.
As though pervaded by darkness
by yakṣas, bhūtas, piśācas and the rest,
by all the vicious ones, grahas and the like,
so they saw this trichiliocosm.
Nothing else at all did they see;
elsewhere the directions were pervaded by darkness.
Bewildered by Vajra Wrath,
stupefied by illusion,
utterly confounded,
they were confounded with one another.
Crying out the sound "Ha ha!"
and uttering the words
"Where have we gone to here?"
they spoke to one another:
"Who is this? And who are we?
Into whose presence have we come?
Have we fallen in our own abodes?
Have we gone to another world?
Have we been slain by Vajra Wrath?
Then what is this?
Just as we do not see the abodes of the gods,
neither do we see the Vajra Bearer.
It is not the wheel of the buddhas' maṇḍala.
We have fallen into darkness.
We do not see our own abodes,
nor do we see Meru itself;
there are no lakes and no oceans,
no stars, no moon, no sun.
From where have we all come?
To what land are we to go?
The five destinies, too, do not exist;
there is no world and there are no mātṛkās;
humans and nonhumans
are not seen in any way at all.
Neither before nor afterward
has such a marvel ever occurred."
Elsewhere the earth quaked,
shaken by the winds;
there was the sound of thunder and great lightning,
and every suffering was present.
Not free of hunger, thirst
and the other sufferings, all became suffering.
There the miraculous powers of the great gods
were not seen in the least.
The hells, the pretas and the animals
were ruined and torn down.
Through the mudrā that tears evil apart,
the three lower realms were stilled.
The gods who dwell without perception
and who live in the formless realm,
their very selves within the five aggregates,
on seeing such a wonder,
examined themselves
and gazed at one another as well.
Likewise all were frightened and terrified:
"What is this wonder that has come about?
For immeasurable great eons
we have dwelt in purity.
We were freed from the supreme self,
yet we have come again to grasp at a self.
Through former habitual tendencies and longing,
we came to see from within the three existences.
Yet the three existences are not seen,
and likewise the five destinies are not there.
Elsewhere, in utter purity, as one,
emanations of Dharma and complete enjoyment,
only the five buddhas are seen.
The abodes of the three existences are nowhere else."
All the assembly became delighted
and spoke to one another:
"Who is this? And who are we?
We have gone to another world.
Before, I saw this world
together with the five destinies,
together with Meru and the ocean,
and the six classes of gods with their retinues.
Which of the gods
of the three existences has seen this?
The celestial palace and the beings
and the Lord we see just as they are.
Such wonders of every kind
are seen in this great abode.
This magical display of the mind of awakening
is the play of all the buddhas."
Even those who are supreme
among all the pratyekabuddhas of this trichiliocosm
were utterly bewildered
and did not see the magical display.
They did not see the Buddha himself,
nor the Victor and the Victor's children;
the dharmadhātu, firm and peaceful,
that abode they did not see.
Elsewhere, this trichiliocosm
was not seen in any way at all:
not the elements of earth, fire and water,
nor the golden disks,
nor the beings of the five destinies,
nor the moon and the sun,
nor the stars, planets and constellations;
nor the beings, nor the three realms.
All these became empty;
elsewhere there was not even the sphere of space.
All of them, frightened and terrified,
attained nirvāṇa.
Whatever fearless śrāvakas there were
in the ten directions,
by the great bodhisattva
they were all terrified.
In each of their own fields
they did not see the Buddha himself,
nor the great assemblies,
nor those who teach the Dharma,
nor the beings of that field,
nor the gods and humans.
There were no houses, there was no field,
no earth, no fire and no wind.
Not seeing even their own fields,
they grew afraid of Vajrasattva.
In an instant they became joyless;
in a moment they became bewildered.
In such a magical display
the mind of awakening shines forth.
In a great eon of the past
the magical display of the wheel was shown.
The Blessed One, the vajra yogin,
blessed it in just this way.
Whatever beings dwell here
no longer knew day from night.
Then that very great secret,
the host firm in the dharmadhātu,
the buddhas of Dharma and complete enjoyment:
such a magical display arose.
The abode of the gods, embracing
the whole trichiliocosm, was arrayed,
the one single abode of the buddhas,
emanation of Dharma and complete enjoyment.
In some places it was beautiful with blue;
the area of the ground was adorned,
ornamented with blue vajras.
In some places it appeared golden in color,
ornamented with the color yellow;
that portion of ground was adorned,
ornamented with yellow vajras.
In some places it was red in color,
beautiful as coral;
that portion of ground was adorned,
ornamented with red vajras.
In some places, ochre in color,
it was beautiful like vaiḍūrya;
that portion of ground was adorned,
made beautiful with madder-colored vajras.
In some places it was crystal in color,
beautiful with brilliant light;
that portion of ground was adorned,
ornamented with crystal vajras.
In the east is great blue,
the south is endowed with yellow,
the western portion is red,
and the north is endowed with madder.
The portions of ground in the center
are crystal-colored, unsurpassed,
utterly pure through the great vajra:
this is taught as the nature of the five colors.
Square, with four gates,
beautiful with four porticoes,
in equal portions, well divided,
endowed with proper sequence.
On every side, in the outer areas,

it was furnished with vajra borders,
endowed with borders all around,
and adorned with the borders of the gates.
Its lines were of the nature of blue vajra,
and others were golden in color,
and of the supreme color red,
and others of excellent crystal,
supreme, of the nature of all jewels.
The maṇḍala, beautiful in its variety,
supreme bearer of various forms,
was honored by the hosts of vajra goddesses,
adorned with nets and half-nets,
beautified with half-moons,
covered all over with nets of small bells,
with supreme bells hanging all around.
It had various delightful trees,
delightful wish-fulfilling trees,
incense, flowers and likewise fragrances,
and was endowed with supreme trees of light,
endowed with arrays of every kind.
Various waters of vajra nectar
filled the supreme lakes with water,
with vast vajra oceans.
Flowers of every kind were strewn all about;
it was perfumed with various incenses,
sprinkled with various scented waters,
pervaded everywhere by various fragrances.
The ten sons of the Great Fierce One,
and then delightful things, arose.
From all the trees, the lotuses and the rest,
the Dharma was heard to come forth.
Spreading forth into all the directions,
in the pure field, together with emptiness,
whatever sound resounds,
the bliss of the Dharma comes about.
yaṃ laṃ śaṃ vaṃ
oṃ vajra satva krodhaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha rāgaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra duṣṭi krodhaya sādhu sādhu hūṃ phaṭ
The Wrathful One, the supreme Vajra Jewel,
with a garland of flames unbearable,
the unsurpassed Wrathful Vajra,
spoke the supreme secret:
oṃ vajra bhṛkuṭi krodha hana hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra sūrya mahā jvala māla krodha jvālaya sarva hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra ketu mahā krodha dhena hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ aṭṭa hāsa vajra maṭṭa hāsa krodha ha ha ha ha hūṃ phaṭ
The great Wrathful One, Vajra Dharma,
pervaded by a garland of flames,
the king of wrathful ones, utterly hard to bear,
spoke the supremely terrifying words:
oṃ vajra dharma viśodhaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ tikṣṇa krodha cchinda hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra hetu krodha praveśaya maṇḍalaṃ sarva hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha bhāṣa ta pāta hūṃ phaṭ
The great king, Vajra Karma,
gathering all the secrets together,
came forth pervaded by brilliant flames,
and the wrathful king spoke:
oṃ vajra karma krodha hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra kavaca krodha rakṣa rakṣa hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra yakṣa krodha khāda khāda hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra krodha muṣṭi sādhaya samaya hūṃ phaṭ
These beings, the great secret,
were transformed from peaceful forms:
the Vajra Wrathful Ones, greatly fierce,
took their seats in the maṇḍala of the wheel.
Then King Vajradhara,
the great Wrathful One of great might,
set forth these secrets in full,
and the offering goddesses spoke them:
oṃ vajra lāsye rāgaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra mālai abhiṣiñcaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra gītye gātaya hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra nṛtye vaśi kuru hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra puṣpa pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra gandha pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra dhūpa pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra ālokai pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ
Then King Vajradhara,
the Wrathful One, fierce and utterly vicious,
Vajra Wrath, exceedingly ferocious:
the gatekeepers came forth on every side.
oṃ aṃkuśa mahā krodha ākarṣaya sarva samaya hūṃ jaḥ
oṃ vajra pāśa mahā krodha praveśaya sarva samaya hūṃ hūṃ
oṃ vajra sphoṭa mahā krodha bandhaya sarva samaya hūṃ vaṃ
oṃ vajra āveśa mahā krodha āveśaya sarva samaya hūṃ phaṭ hoḥ
The wheel, perfectly round,
became the great Vajradhara,
pervaded by the hosts of great wrathful ones,
endowed with the great maṇḍala,
endowed with the great abode.
Gathered all together into the great maṇḍala:
the utterly secret great Dharma,
emanation of Dharma and complete enjoyment,
that very essence, the great secret,
the great liberation, the supreme city,
the great king of maṇḍalas,
endowed with every aim of sentient beings,
the dharmatā fully awakened:
such is Dharma and complete enjoyment.
Whatever is the secret of the five families,
that which acts without any doubt:
the Tathāgata family is peaceful,
because it is the great host of the pure.
Likewise the Vajra family is fierce,
because it thoroughly tames the Great God (Śiva),
emanating the Great God and the rest.
Likewise there is the supreme Lotus family,
because, for the beings to be tamed,
emanations of every kind truly arise.
The wheel of emanation is peaceful,
encircled by fierce offerings.
The wheel of the various families
is endowed with the four families.
The supreme Tathāgata family
arose as the eastern wheel.
Then the fierce Vajra family
is truly terrifying for the sake of beings.
This great supreme Vajra family
gathers in all the hosts of the maṇḍala;
the great king, Sovereign of Secrets,
because he is declared the Lord of Secrets.
Gathering all the vajras together,
having explained his own supreme family,
the great King Vajradhara
emanated the great maṇḍala in full.
All the vicious ones, the gods and the rest,
he summoned in that very instant,
and having then made them enter,
in an instant he struck them with the pledge.
Having conferred the empowerment of the vajra sign
and given them what is endowed with the pledge,
having summoned them and made them fully enter,
likewise he bound them and brought them under control.
They were made to strive in the pledge;
and so it is with the pledge and the vow.

For the benefit of human beings
the supreme maṇḍala was proclaimed:
the great wheel, the great city,
the great and supreme Vajra family,
called Trailokyavijaya.
This I shall now explain correctly.
Having vanquished the hosts of the three worlds,
the gods and the rest were made to enter.
Then the abode of Trailokyavijaya,
the great wheel, the great city:
by merely taking up its name,
beings are utterly subdued,
and grahas and the like are put to flight.
Fevers, grahas and poisons,
the fierce and vicious ones, and those of the pledge,
at the very moment of that excellent name,
are terrified and tremble.
The body is the supreme Body of Complete Enjoyment;
but there are humans who are not to be tamed by it,
and therefore the king Vajradhara
emanated the great maṇḍala.
The vajra master is of great fortune,
thoroughly trained and highly learned,
knowing the rites of the intention,
having heard the texts and skilled in their meaning,
deeply faithful, intelligent and steady,
knowing well what is fitting and unfitting,
diligent in the mind of awakening,
without desire for other gods,
having seen the supreme secret essence,
compassionate by nature and loving,
pure, gentle and fully disciplined,
abiding in his own natural state,
not clinging, attached or vicious,
without delusion and without laziness,
loving, with a mind intent on benefit,
always delighting in mirth.
Having fully overcome attachment
to form and the other qualities of desire,
his mind is afraid of existence,
and he has no clinging to existence.
He is diligent in the way of the Great Vehicle,
without desire for other vehicles,
diligent for the aims of all beings,
sprung from the supreme of the Three Jewels.
Abandoning all attachment to the afflictions,
he thoroughly cultivates their antidotes:
for desire and the other great afflictions
he cultivates the unattractive and the rest.
He knows the rites of the fitting and the unfitting,
and is highly skilled in mantras and mudrās:
summoning, causing to enter, binding,
bringing under control, and requesting departure,
as they are explained in the Vajradhātu.
First the vidyā observance is to be performed.
One must be wary in every respect
of even the slightest fault.
The measurements, the colored powders, the cord,
and the implements are to accord with the rite.
The binding, and the striking with the kīla,
are just as explained before in the Vajradhātu.
What is to be eaten and what is not to be eaten,
through the samādhi of vajra pride,
in the Vajradhātu and the other maṇḍalas,
are to be done just as before.
Then I shall explain it fully and correctly:
the great maṇḍala, which is supreme,
entirely like the Vajradhātu,
supreme in bringing about all accomplishments.
It is square, with four gates,
adorned with four porticoes,
furnished with four lines,
and adorned with tassels of silk.
At whatever size is fitting,
just like the great vajra city,
entirely like the Vajradhātu,
one should draw the city of Vajradhara,
furnished with five wheels
and furnished with eight pillars.
In its center, in the middle
of its natural wheel, place the Buddha:
Vairocana, the great Teacher,
Vajrasattva of great light,
the bodhisattvas, the great kings:
the maṇḍala of the buddhas' complete enjoyment.
In front of the Buddha, the vajra
is to be placed in the midst of flames:
blazing like Vajra Fire,
the mudrā that conquers the three worlds.
On the right, the jewel mudrā
is to be placed in the midst of flames.
On the left, draw the lotus mudrā.
Behind, the sharp vajra
is to be placed in the midst of flames.
These are the four kings of mudrās,
endowed with blazing fire.
In the great maṇḍala of Vairocana,
the great city of the dharmadhātu,
the mudrās of the four
wheel-turning kings are also to be placed.
Whatever lines are to be drawn,
the wise one should strike the lines of the maṇḍala.
With this vajra mantra
take up the vajra cord fully:
oṃ vajra samaya sūtraṃ mātikrama hūṃ
Having gone with vajra speed
through the great condensed maṇḍala,
one should draw and place the king
who embraces all the vajra mudrās.
oṃ vajra vegākrama hūṃ
Having all the colored powders ready,
gaze on everything with a blazing gaze;
spread the vajra colors
and urge on the vajra mantra:
oṃ vajra citra samaya hūṃ
Wherever a mudrā is to be placed,
all the heart mantras are to be placed.
Whatever was placed before
encircles the vajra form.
In the maṇḍala of Vajrahūṃkara,
having gone with vajra speed:
the vajra vidyā, the supreme king,
unshakable, in perfect equipoise,
four-faced and eight-armed,
his fangs slightly bared,
the king of wrathful ones, pervaded by flames,
his hands bearing weapons along with mudrās.
The mudrā of Vajrahūṃkara:
two hands are held above;
likewise, two hands
hold raised hooks;
one hand holds a noose,
one hand holds a vajra;
and two hands are fully set
in the gesture of drawing an arrow.
This is the mudrā of Vajrahūṃkara:
he tramples Maheśvara completely,
and with his right foot presses down
on the goddess Umā between her breasts.
In all the directions around him
place the Vajra Wrathful Ones,
pervaded by garlands of flame and blazing,
wrathful ones supreme in inspiring terror.
In front is Vajrasattva,
on the right is Vajrarāja,
on the left is Vajrarāga,
and behind is Vajrasādhu.
Having gone with vajra speed,
in the south, the maṇḍala of the wrathful one:
draw the king of wrathful ones, Bhṛkuṭi,
surrounded by wrathful ones,
all pervaded by garlands of flame,
his face wrathful and laughing,
endowed with the mudrā of Vajreśvara:
he is proclaimed Bhṛkuṭi.
In all the directions around him
are the great Vajra Wrathful Ones;
gathering their mantras and mudrās,
draw them correctly in their own places.
In front is Vajraratna,
on the right the Wrathful Radiance,
on the left the Fierce Banner,
and behind the Wrathful Laughter.
Having gone with vajra speed,
in the western quarter, the maṇḍala:
the great Wrathful Vajra,
surrounded by the great lords of wrath.
Vajradharma is in front,
Vajratīkṣṇa on the right,
likewise Vajrahetu on the left,
and Vajrabhāṣa behind.

Having gone, too, with vajra speed, the fourth of the supreme maṇḍalas: the great Vajra Descent of action, surrounded by the great wrathful ones. Vajra Karma is in front, on the right is Vajrarakṣa, on the left Vajrayakṣa, and behind Vajramuṣṭi. In the four corners of the central wheel draw correctly the four goddesses. In the corners, upon moon discs, draw Vajralāsyā and the others. In the corners of the outer maṇḍala draw Vajrapuṣpā and the others. As in the Vajradhātu and the other maṇḍalas, following the rite explained before, at the four gates, in every case, the gatekeepers are to be placed: the Hook, the Noose, the Chain, and the Bell, the unsurpassed descent. Their own mudrās are placed at the heart, with each one's own heart mantra. With vajra and bell, endowed with delight, set out Vairocana. Oneself is Vajra Wrath, the lord, the supreme Vajra Vidyā; as Vajrarāja, bless it supremely: oneself is the supreme vajra maṇḍala. Having drawn the maṇḍala in this way, enter with the vajra tirintiri. Having examined it correctly, circumambulate it seven times. Whatever is lacking is to be made wholly pure; the wise one should draw in what is further needed. The vajra bell is to be understood; it becomes accomplished, without fault. With Vajra Hook and all the others, the rites are performed in meditative equipoise. Having entered the middle of the wheel, the wise one should open the gates. With the mind lifting the vajra lines and opening them completely, the four vajra gates are to be opened with the mind. Its heart mantra is this: oṃ vajradhara udghaṭaya praveśaya samaya hūṃ In the places outside the maṇḍala draw the outer vajra families. The gods, Viṣṇu and the rest, abide in half of the enclosure, with their wheels and with their families: vicious beings, grahas and the like, fever spirits, bhūtas and piśācas, ḍākinīs, bhūtas and mātṛkās, and all the goddesses bearing weapons; draw them just as one wishes. In the places of the outer enclosure are the beautiful vajra enclosures. Then, first, the eminent master, seated in the middle of the maṇḍala, in the vajra cross-legged posture, enters into absorption in samādhi. Just as the vajra family arose, so should one contemplate oneself. Supreme king of vajra vidyā, bless yourself correctly. Supreme king, Vajra Wrath: in a samādhi such as this, through the samādhi of Vajrasattva, the one of great fortune enters absorption, and, just as was proclaimed above, the wise one contemplates it all. How the vajra family arises, how the maṇḍala is completed, the vast and great secret, and the supreme rite of the vajra master, the great tantra called the Secret, are fully explained in this ritual section. Then, having taken his seat in the center, the vajra master of great austerity, binding the mudrā of the wrathful one, binds all the mudrās completely. Binding the Wrathful Vajra Hook, he should invite all the buddhas. The sacred hundred and eight names should be recited once. With the offerings of Vajra Flower and the rest all the guides should be worshiped. Whatever secret offering is to be made, all the mudrās are bound completely. Endowed with mudrās and secondary mudrās, the offerings likewise themselves, and the answering mudrās and all the rest, are presented fully in accordance with the rite. The vajra bell is to be rung, and the maṇḍala of the vicious threatened. The vicious mind is to be tamed, and the afflictions utterly slain. The descent and the recitation are to be performed. Worship is to be made with vajra offerings. Duly endowed with song and offering, the vajra song is to be sung. Singing these songs of the vajra, one should please the supreme ones. Bowing down, one should make the offering; the vajra song is to be sung: śata śata matasta tiri ṭata ṭata briti hata mata śata brata kata tabiṭa biṭa ṭa ṭa ṭa baṭa iti taṭa ṭa ṭa kara ṭara ṭara raṭa With the mudrā of the vajra tirintiri one should empower oneself. Having received permission as one wishes, urge on the vajra mantra. The upper body is to be covered with a blue cloth, and the eyes covered with blue. With the mudrā of the vajra tirintiri bind the vajra disciple: oṃ gṛhna vajra samaya hūṃ vaṃ He is to be led in as is fitting, and the vajra mantra urged on. With this mantra of the vajra the vajra disciple is to be led in: oṃ vajra samaya praveśeyāmi As in the Vajradhātu and the other maṇḍalas, in full, just as before, the rites of the descent of wisdom are to be performed by the wise.

The offerings and the torma
are just as the Vajra Bearer taught before.
The wise one should train in them all
and perform them with a clear and confident mind.
With the mudrā of Vajrahūṃkara
the wise one should accomplish hūṃ.
All the vicious ones, gods and the rest,
are to be summoned and brought in there.
One should train in the vajra yoga,
and oneself, or else another,
is to be empowered with the vajra garland.
With the mudrā of the vajra oath
the oath is first to be proclaimed.
Vajra wisdom, great wisdom:
vajra wisdom is to be fully accomplished.
Holding the vajra emblem in the hand,
the vajra mantra is to be fully accomplished.
With this mantra of the vajra
the vajra emblem is bestowed:
oṃ vajrapāṇi karmakārodbhāva
Then the name empowerment is conferred,
and the vajra mantra urged on.
With this mantra of the vajra
the vajra name empowerment is conferred:
oṃ vajrakrodhastvaṃ abhiṣiñcāmi
oṃ vajranāma abhiṣekata he vajranāma
The perfect arising of wisdom
is to be meditated upon by the wise.
Trained through supreme wisdom,
one fully accomplishes vajra yoga.
Having drawn a vajra at the heart,
fully endowed with the various rites,
pervaded by a garland of flames and blazing,
the vajra at the heart is to be meditated upon.
Supreme king of Vajra Vidyā:
so should one fully meditate oneself.
The king who holds all the rites:
so should one fully meditate oneself.
Or else, as one among the wrathful ones,
so should one fully meditate oneself.
Through absorption as Vajra Wrath
all beings are to be made to receive the descent:
hūṃ satva vajrakrodha āveśa aḥ
Through the samādhi of the wrathful jewel,
draw a vajra jewel correctly.
Oneself being Vajraratna,
bring all the buddhas under one's power.
Having bound the vajra lotus at the neck,
draw it correctly at the throat.
Having meditated oneself as Vajradharma,
by that one destroys all afflictions.
A vajra is to be drawn at the crown of the head,
and the holder of the blazing vajra set there.
Having meditated oneself as Vajra Karma,
all the vicious ones are to be dispelled.
ho vajraratna krodha vaśamānāyatra
hriḥ vajradharma krodha nāśaya phaṭ
hūṃ karmavajra krodha rakṣa
The divine host, all the buddhas,
are to be summoned together with their mudrās.
The wisdom is blessed and then held,
and in just that way it is correctly blessed.
Drawing a vajra hook on the hand,
the threatening forefinger bearing the syllable hūṃ,
the wrathful one moves his finger:
this is supreme for summoning the gods.
oṃ vajrakrodha ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ
Likewise below, on the sole of the foot,
draw a vajra hook correctly.
Whatever god one presses down with it,
this is the supreme means of summoning him.
oṃ vajrakrodha agra aṃkuśa ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ
Duly endowed with an outstretched arm,
having correctly drawn a vajra hook,
whatever god it presses upon,
each of them is supremely summoned.
oṃ vajrakrodhana ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ
Having correctly drawn a vajra hook
upon the seat, one presses down on it,
and whatever god is pressed down by it,
each of them is supremely summoned.
oṃ vajrakrodha āsana āgacchaya ākarṣaya mite he vajrasamya manusmara hūṃ phaṭ
With the mudrā of descent, cause the descent correctly;
the wise one should train in wisdom.
With all the vajra applications
the practices, too, are correctly undertaken.
Standing in the posture with the left leg outstretched,
one should meditate oneself as the wrathful one.
Swiftly, without any delay,
cause the wisdom of the buddhas to descend.
oṃ vajrakrodha kāya āveśa hūṃ phaṭ
Applying Vajrahūṃkara correctly,
one should meditate oneself as the wrathful one.
Summon correctly the awakening of the buddhas,
and urge on the vajra mantra.
oṃ vajravidyottama mahākrodha ānaya ho bhagavan vajra hūṃ phaṭ
Meditating oneself as the wrathful one,
one should look with the wrathful gaze.
In the terrifying prison of saṃsāra
all sentient beings are to be slain.
oṃ dṛṣṭa mahākrodha maraya sarvasatva hūṃ
Always, as one clad in armor,
one should meditate oneself with the mind,
and by meditating others too in this way,
all these beings will be protected.
oṃ vajra rakṣa rakṣa sarva satva hūṃ phaṭ
The secret mudrā, the great yoga,
is to be trained in, in four ways.
Vajra wisdom, the great reality:
contemplate the wisdom of the buddhas' awakening.
Through absorption as Vajra Wrath,
toward whatever being one is attached,
one should meditate that form with the mind
and fully recite the syllable hūṃ.
Through these vajra applications,
with the mudrā of the vajra ḍākinī,
whatever being one is wholly attached to,
that one's afflictions are destroyed.
ṭakki asalave alave accha ṭakki hūṃ phaṭ
Even by reciting this a single time,
the afflictions melt away and are shaken.
ṭakki ja
Whatever being's mind stream
is looked upon with the vajra gaze,
by the great eye of vajra yoga
that one's suffering is destroyed.
ṭakki hūṃ
From the transformation of the syllable hūṃ,
whatever being is near at hand,
at that one's heart, too, a vajra
the wise one imagines with the mind.
Then the mudrā of not turning back
from all fears, as well,
is to be sealed with the vajra yoga ḍākinī
and with the vajra mudrā.
ṭakki ho
Vajra yoga, great and wondrous,
vajra yoga, whatever is vajra,
the maṇḍala of the yoga ḍākinīs:
the wise one blesses the supreme secret.
Through the union of the two organs,
embracing completely with all the limbs,
the one who applies the syllable hūṃ
destroys suffering.
Holding the most fierce Vajra Wrath,
one should always meditate oneself.
Whatever is the wrathful body of that one
is the single, supremely secret self.
Be it a buddha or a bodhisattva,
as equal to oneself:
this correct joining of bodies
is to be declared the union of the organs.

Whatever is the body of a buddha or bodhisattva, or of Vajra Wrath or another being, and whatever is my own body, to know these as equal: this is called the union of the two organs.

Or else, by way of meditation,
one embraces the limbs as was explained.
Vajrasattva, the great king,
should be held fast in the arms.
By embracing completely with all the limbs,
the awakening of the buddhas is fully accomplished.
In the union of the two organs
the passion of desire is not to be entertained.
By meditating on equality through this,
equality itself is the organ.
With the teeth of the application of haṃ traṃ hūṃ,
holding the lip, one kisses,
and whatever being one meditates upon,
by that one obtains a bliss that is not in vain.
hūṃ ja
Bringing bliss to all sentient beings,
fully applying that syllable hūṃ,
having purified oneself as the wrathful one,
one then attains bliss.
hūṃ
Whoever fully applies hūṃ,
and if, by whatever beings,
all the limbs are drawn together,
their afflictions become thinned.
hūṃ phaṭ
Endowed with means through great wisdom,
the whole of the vajra family,
likewise together with the retinue of the wheel,
is to be trained in by the wise.
Through the samādhi of union with the buddhas
one should always meditate oneself.
Endowed with all the supreme ripenings,
meditate on the body of a buddha.
Through that application of meditation
one attains buddhahood in an instant.

Supreme king of vajra vidyā:
so should one fully meditate oneself.
hūṃ

One should meditate oneself as Vajrarāja.

vaṃ
Whatever one desires,
in just the way the deity of that family appears,
so should one meditate oneself;
through this it is accomplished in an instant.
hūṃ
Binding once with the vajra mudrā,
the wise one performs the vajra recitation,
as was explained in the tantra of recitation;
otherwise one should adopt the wrathful one.
One should recite with the mind as well,
and urge it on with the mudrā of the body.
By joining the three maṇḍalas equally,
the wise one accomplishes it in that instant.
Then the delightful vajra family,
the countless great mudrās,
duly endowed with the mudrās of the body,
because beings are not fit vessels,
for that reason were taught only briefly.
The full compendium of the great mudrā
is fully explained, from the Compendium of the Great Vehicle,
in that tantra,
the great tantra called the Secret.
With this secret rite
the work of yoga is to be accomplished.
Through the application of vajra yoga
buddhahood is accomplished in an instant.
Here the many benefits
are to be applied with certainty to one's own practice.
Whoever is disciplined and applies himself fully
accomplishes buddhahood in an instant.
Even one who commits the five deeds of immediate retribution,
having entered, becomes diligent,
and if he performs the rite of yoga,
there is no doubt that he will succeed.
In this very life the yogin
attains the ground of Great Joy.
Whoever applies himself to the Vajra Bearer
will without doubt attain the grounds.
The hardships of countless eons
are not without the very nature of practice;
yet for one who strives in this yoga
the grounds are therefore not hard to find.
This Great Vehicle is secret;
it teaches the way of the Dharma of the buddhas,
the great essence of the buddhas' awakening;
from it the grounds are not hard to attain.
Standing in the posture with the left leg outstretched,
proclaim the wrathful words.
Truly wrathful, with the wrathful gaze,
all the rites are to be accomplished.

From the Vajra Peak, the king of ritual of the supreme secret, the sacred reality, the secret yoga, lord of the sūtras of all the tathāgatas, the explanation called Victory over the Three Realms, the third chapter, is complete. From the Vajra Family, the first chapter.

Then the supreme compendium of all the secrets,
truly arisen from union with the joy of mind,
its work accomplished and supremely beautiful,
the host of all the emanations of Vajreśvara.
Then the supreme great family of the buddhas,
arisen from the heart of Vajragarbha and endowed with bliss,
gathering the supreme qualities of the utmost secret:
Vajrarāja took on the form of a woman.
"How wonderful! The woman who enjoys supreme delight,
arisen from the compendium of all the secrets,
is Vajrasattva, supremely free of fear."
Having said this, he remained silent.

The supreme one, truly arisen from the pledge of dhāraṇī,
abiding in samādhi, the secret of the nature of reality,
from the teaching that gathers all, from the crown to the soles of the feet,
drew forth the hosts of the maṇḍala of his family.
hrīḥ
Then he drew forth, too, those whose names are joy,
the supreme vajra women arisen from his heart,
far surpassing the gods of the supreme form realm;
in the vajra quarters the words of the goddesses resound.
Joyful, with smiling eyes,
with faces laughing and flirtatious,
goddesses of the gods of the families of Brahmā and the rest,
delightful, victorious through great joy.
That goddess Vajrayoginī, too,
abiding in the center of the wheel, contemplates;
the gathering of her family, beautiful and delightful,
is the great vajra lady, the Vajra Bewilderer.
oṃ sarva tathāgata mahārāga mohanaya hi
This heart mantra was spoken by the bewilderer of the buddhas.
Even the supreme tathāgatas become bewildered.
She is the joy that bewilders the joy of all the buddhas,
supreme in wholly transforming the forms of the buddhas.
That supreme lord of sages, Vairocana,
becomes an emanation in the form of a woman.
That lord who possesses supreme miraculous power
became the four sisters of the retinue.
oṃ vajrakrodha samaye sthi
Then the peaceful host of steadfast secrets,
pervading all with the body of Dharma and the body of enjoyment,
the Vajra Sovereign of Secrets, master of bewildering,
abides in the pure nature of reality of the buddhas' consorts.
The earth quaked in six ways.
Rays of light of many kinds shone in the ten directions.
The goddesses, outshining
the goddesses together with the gods, became greatly expanded.
The hosts of goddesses, equal to the maṇḍalas of the families,
abide in their own celestial palaces.
Then, of the buddha Ratnasambhava,
the sacred Sovereign of Secrets, the guide,
set out the great Vajra Joy.
The syllable of the lord of all the vicious,
arisen from the heart, the pure nature of reality:
the four vajra consorts and the central one, five in all,
abide in the eastern portion of the maṇḍala host,
the sacred and supreme lord of Akṣobhya.
Then, arisen from the one of the vajra family,
the eastern maṇḍala, with the wrathful ones and with its maṇḍala,
the one whose nature is the pledge of sacred Akṣobhya
spoke the urging of the heart mantras;
he spoke the arising of the most supreme heart mantras:
oṃ vajrakrodha samaye ji
oṃ vajraroṣa aṃkuśa ānaya sarva ji
oṃ vajraroṣa karmavajri vaśaṃ me ānaya di ji
oṃ vajraduṣṭi krodha tiṣṭhaya sarva ji
That Ratnasambhava, in the form of a woman,
that supremely delightful jewel,
spoke the four heart mantras of the retinue:
oṃ vajrabhṛkuṭī krodha hara hara sarva garbha ji
oṃ vajrajvalamāla praveśa mahākrodha agni jvālaya sarva rabhiṣaja ji
oṃ vajradhvaja agra keyuri mahākrodhi dehi sarva ji
oṃ vajra aṭṭāṭṭa hasini hāsamayāṭṭāṭṭa hara hara ji
The syllable ji abides most firmly;
he spoke the heart mantra that had become supreme.
That sacred lord of jewels
spoke the supreme ones that urge on the heart mantras;
he spoke the supreme heart mantra, he spoke the wondrous one.
Amitābha, having taken the form of a woman,
supreme source of the sacred vajra consorts,
spoke the four heart mantras:
oṃ vajraśuddhi hana duṣṭi dhi
oṃ tīkṣṇa krodha cchindana sarva dhiri
vajrahetu mahākrodha braveśaya sarva dhi
oṃ vajrajihvā mahākrodha bhāṣi paca uṅgaraya dhiḥ
He spoke the steadfast heart mantra of supreme knowledge.
The supreme lotus maṇḍala, endowed with power,
showing the supreme secret of Amitābha,
spoke the supreme heart mantra of urging:
ṇi
With great joy he drew forth the emblems as well.
Amoghasiddhi, having taken the form of a woman,
endowed with intense urging toward action,
spoke the four sisters of the retinue:
oṃ sarvamukhe karmavajriṇi mahākrodha kuru sarva ṇi
oṃ vajrakavaca krodhi rakṣa maṇi
oṃ vajrakrodhe mahāyakṣiṇi vajre daṃṣṭā karāli vilāsa sarva ṇi
oṃ vajramuṣṭi vajrakrodhani
Then the various maṇḍalas of action,
the supremely secret compendium, the great joy,
urging on the maṇḍala of the central lord,
spoke this supreme heart mantra of the consort of action:
hūṃ
From the center the various secrets come to arise;
Vairocana spoke these chief ones:
oṃ vajreśvarī hūṃ
oṃ ratneśvarī hūṃ
oṃ padmeśvarī hūṃ
oṃ karmeśvarī hūṃ
Whatever arose from the urging, together with the Vajra Queen,
abides, through the four yogas of the vajra consorts, as the central sovereign.
The consorts of vajra, jewel and Dharma, endowed with beautiful limbs:
apart from this secret host there is no supreme yoga at all.
Vajrasattva, supremely free of fear,
abides in the center of his own retinue.
With a mind of love and of benefit
he spoke the supreme vajra secret maṇḍala.
Then, having shown the city of the wheel,
he whose nature is Victory over the Three Realms
correctly drew the secret yoga of the maṇḍala
of the consort of the lord of vajra yoga.
Draw the city correctly in two ways;
the wheel is to be arranged in two ways.
Likewise, enter correctly into this city,
or else draw it correctly just as one wishes.
And together with the host of mudrās,
draw correctly the vajra worthy of meditation.
Or else, worshiped with the secret Dharma,
now draw correctly what is endowed with form.
In the central tier sits Vajrasattva,
endowed with the noose and with the mudrās of the retinue.
From the blazing vajra, jewel and lotus,
place them blazing and endowed with fire.
The eastern maṇḍala, the great nature of the five wheels:
in the vajra maṇḍala, where Akṣobhya is chief,
sporting with the great vajra mudrā,
the supreme one, trampling Maheśvara who bears the trident.
Then, in the places of the four directions,
the Vajra Hook, the arrow and the joy,
fully endowed with rays of blazing fire,
possessing mudrās that abide in the midst of the blaze.
That one, delightful in the jewel family:
in the center of the vajra sits Vajraratna.
Then, in the places of the four directions,
the mudrās of the jewel, the radiance, the banner and the laughter.

Likewise the lotus family, delightful,
with the mudrā of the vajra lotus;
then, in the quarters of the four directions,
the lotus, the sword, the wheel and likewise speech.
Likewise, from that family wrought by action,
Vajratīkṣṇa, endowed with great fire;
firmly in the quarters of its four directions
are Karma, Yakṣa, Rakṣa and Muṣṭi.
From that, among the vast hosts of forms,
the supreme and fierce ḍākinīs of the vajra consorts,
terrifying, with great blazing fangs;
in the center is the vajra sun, ablaze.
Together with the rites for protecting the ground,
one should always look into the vast supreme scripture.
All the rites of purification and of descent
are to be taken from the sacred one endowed with the supreme limbs.
Then train in the mudrās and the limbs of the mudrās.
With the offering mudrās, the offering mudrās are declared.
Then, in the maṇḍala with its gates and corners,
summon the consorts, the meteoric iron, the supreme yakṣa.
Then, for the host entering at the gates:
vajra jaḥ jaḥ
the vast cloud of the summoning strokes;
vajra hūṃ hūṃ
likewise the one who grants the dharma of binding;
vajra vaṃ vaṃ
then the manifold nature of reality of wisdom;
vajra aḥ aḥ
then worship together with secret grace;
vajra hūṃ khaṃ
then endowed with the various garlands of the limbs;
vajra hūṃ ghuṃ
then the singing of songs and music;
vajra hūṃ taṃ
likewise the other supreme lords of the maṇḍala;
vajra hūṃ ste
then, outside, the vast flowers;
vajra tite
likewise thousands of instruments struck;
vajra ṭraṃ ta
the drawing of incense, fragrances and music;
vajra dhātu
then rays of light and radiance fully streaming forth.
vajra krodha
Then summon with the host of the Vajra Hook,
and please them with joy, fully endowed with the vajra.
oṃ siddhiya vajra
ānaya vajra
Then the ground of passion, the one full of desire:
rāgaya vajra ahi vajra
likewise the one who gazes at the sun:
jvālaya vajra
of the banner:
dehi vajra
of the laughter:
hāsa vajra
likewise of attachment:
kāmaya vajra
of grasping the sword:
cchedaya vajra
and further, of turning:
bhramaya vajra
then of uttering the host of words:
brūhi vajra
the steadfast host of the maṇḍala of the offerings of action:
tyi vajra
then of the portion of the rite:
rakṣa vajra
likewise of eating:
khāhi vajra
the delightful body is to be firmly bound:
bandhaya vajra
Then likewise extend those of wisdom.
Then urge them on with a wrathful mind.
The lower lip is to be clenched with the teeth,
and likewise the eyes are to be closed.
Then fully meditate the samādhi of the wrathful one.
With the secret mudrās the four māras are vanquished.
Then recite the heart of the vajra words,
and train in the hosts of mudrās of many kinds.
The hosts of the mudrās of the body,
from the Compendium of the Great Vehicle,
in accordance with the command of Vajrasattva,
are fully declared at length.
The Supreme Secret is complete.

Then King Vajradhara,
the lord of the place supreme over all,
abiding in the subtle vajra,
spoke the subtle vajra:
oṃ sarva tathāgata sūkṣma vajra krodha krama hūṃ phaṭ
One should imagine oneself
in the form of a buddha, for the sake of benefit.
Through this subtle vajra
the mind becomes peace itself.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra krodha krama hūṃ phaṭ
Supreme king of vajra vidyā:
so should one fully meditate oneself.
Through this subtle vajra
one's own mind is tamed in an instant.
oṃ sūkṣma vajraratna krodha kramaka hūṃ phaṭ
Oneself as Vajraratna
is to be meditated upon by the wise.
One swiftly attains the Dharma of the buddhas
and becomes the chief, supreme among all jewels.
oṃ sūkṣma vajradharma krodha krama hūṃ phaṭ
Meditate oneself as Vajradharma,
pure from the very beginning.
Through this mudrā of speech
one becomes equal to Vajradharma.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra karma krodha krama hūṃ phaṭ
Oneself as Vajra Karma
is to be meditated upon again and again;
urged on by the vajra mantra,
one becomes equal to Vajra Karma.
The great being of empowerment:
if one always meditates oneself
as the four universal monarchs,
one also obtains the empowerment.
hūṃ oṃ sūkṣma jñāna krodha hūṃ phaṭ
If one meditates oneself as Vajrarāja,
one becomes rigid.
Through these very heart mantras
one summons all the buddhas completely.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra aṃkuśa ākarṣaya mahākrodha hūṃ phaṭ
With the application of drawing the bow,
Vajrarāga is to be meditated upon.
Through these very heart mantras
all the buddhas become attached.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra krodha anurāgaya hūṃ phaṭ
Having pleased all the buddhas,
one should meditate oneself as well.
Being Vajra Joy oneself,
one attains the vajra accomplishment.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra duṣṭi krodha hūṃ phaṭ
I am Vajraratna, the king,
supreme in steadfast empowerment.
Having urged on with the vajra mantra,
one becomes equal to Vajraratna.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra bhṛkuṭī krodha hara hara hūṃ phaṭ
Through the samādhi of Vajratejas
one should always make oneself blaze.
Radiating fully in all directions,
urge on the vajra mantra.
oṃ sūkṣma jvāla maṇḍala krodha sūrya jvala hūṃ phaṭ
As the jewel banner of giving
one should always imagine oneself.
All sentient beings are to be purified,
and the vajra mantra fully urged on.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra dhvaja krodha yaśodha sarva āḥ hūṃ me prayaccha hūṃ phaṭ
Laughing the laughter of the great Dharma,
Vajrahāsa, the steadfast self,
is to be urged on with the vajra mantra;
oneself alone is the speech of Dharma.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra hasa krodha ha ha hūṃ phaṭ ha ha
Peaceful from the very beginning, unborn,
all dharmas are utterly pure.
Oneself as Vajradharma, the king,
recite once with the vajra words.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra krodha yaśodha hūṃ phaṭ
The host of mighty swords,
Vajratīkṣṇa, most steadfast.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra ccheda krodha cchinda cchinda hūṃ phaṭ
Having turned the wheel of Dharma,
one becomes equal to the vajra wheel.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra krodha mahāṃ cakra cchindapa hā iśvari praveśa hṛdayaṃ cchinda hūṃ phaṭ
Now, it is neither what expresses nor what is expressed;

I am the steadfast Vajrabhāṣa.
Having made firm the very longing for the Dharma,
one attains Vajrabhāṣa.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra hūṃ kāra krodha hara pacaya va mudreṇa hūṃ phaṭ
One should worship all the buddhas
and contemplate Vajra Karma as steadfast.
If one teaches the Dharma with vajra words,
one attains Vajra Karma.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra karma sarva kārya hṛdaya hūṃ
I am the king of great loving-kindness,
the steadfast lord Vajrarakṣa.
Having always meditated on oneself as him,
all the lower destinies are destroyed.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra kavaca krodha rakṣa hūṃ phaṭ
One should devour all evil deeds
and meditate on Vajrarakṣa.
If one spurs on the vajra mantra,
the vajra destroys all afflictions.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra yakṣa hara bhakṣa sarva duṣṭa vajra yakṣa hūṃ phaṭ
I am Vajramuṣṭi, the king.
Recollect the mind of awakening.
If one binds all the obstructors,
one becomes equal to Vajramuṣṭi.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra muṣṭi bandha bandha hūṃ phaṭ
That mind at the tip of the nose,
having made it firm, one should properly begin.
Having applied the very syllable hūṃ,
all afflictions are utterly destroyed.
hūṃ
First, at the heart and at the tip of the nose,
having made firm its nature,
if one emanates that sign,
enemies such as disease are destroyed.
nāśaya vajrī hūṃ
Having also made that mind firm,
the holder of yoga should emanate it,
and the wise one should take up the lord of wrath:
negative tendencies are certainly destroyed.
vināśaya sarva vajra hūṃ
For as long as the yogin who knows wishes,
if he then withdraws that sign,
all evil without remainder
is destroyed and does not arise.
oṃ sūkṣma vajra pratya śīghāṃ hūṃ
Clearly, to all directions without exception,
emanate loving-kindness toward all sentient beings.
For as long as one emanates the vajra,
for that long sentient beings will be happy.
bherava vajra krodha hūṃ phaṭ
Alas, these beings who have no protector!
To protect them from fear,
one should emanate that mind of compassion:
one attains vajra wisdom.
kāruṇā vajra krodha hūṃ phaṭ
The essence of all the tathāgatas:
all sentient beings are pure from the beginning.
How wonderful, bliss! How wonderful, the Dharma!
Emanate it fully throughout all directions.
hūṃ kāra viśuddha hūṃ phaṭ
Focusing on the sentient beings of saṃsāra,
practice equanimity together with bliss.
Through this application of equanimity,
emanate it fully toward sentient beings.
oṃ viśodhaya vajra krodha hūṃ phaṭ
Properly draw the form of a vajra.
Whatever it is firmly placed upon,
by that very means the yogin
destroys the whole host of stains.
hūṃ vajra pravarta
Upon whomever the mind of awakening itself
is brought down through recollection,
that sentient being will attain
the very awakening of a buddha.
hūṃ bodhisatva pravarta
Having recollected Vajrapāṇi
and brought down the pure nature of reality,
that sentient being will attain
supreme bliss, without doubt.
hūṃ vajra dhāra pravarta
Meditate on the form of the Buddha
endowed with all the supreme aspects.
If one brings down the qualities of the Buddha,
fear will certainly not arise.
hūṃ buddha pravarta
The Moon, the actual nature of the vajra:
if one meditates on it as it was generated,
the one of excellent mind, equal to the vajra Moon,
will swiftly travel through the sky.
buddha pravartaya hūṃ
Meditating on oneself in the center of the Moon,
those sentient beings upon whom one brings it down
will become irreversible;
of this there is no doubt.
satva pravartaya hūṃ
The learned one should meditate
on oneself as Vajrapāṇi,
and the place where it is brought down
will increase in glory and happiness
very swiftly, without doubt.
vajra satva pravartaya hūṃ
One should meditate in meditative equipoise
on oneself in the form of the Buddha.
Wherever that vajra is brought down
becomes vast in miraculous power.
buddha pravartaya hūṃ
Through the absorption of Vajra Wrath,
encircling one's own body,
the one in whose name this is done
attains awakening without doubt.
oṃ vajra valita krodha māraya hūṃ
In breathing out and breathing in,
fully apply the syllable hūṃ,
and through the absorption of Vajra Wrath
destroy the faults such as desire.
oṃ vajra śvāsa vivātala hūṃ phaṭ
One should look with the vajra gaze,
and by fully applying Vajra Wrath,
whatever sentient being one looks upon
is liberated from saṃsāra.
oṃ vajra dṛṣī vināśaya hūṃ phaṭ
Abiding as the very self of body and speech,
as one's own mind goes forth,
if one recollects the mind of awakening,
by that too one attains buddhahood.
hūṃ hṛdaya ākarṣaya vajra krodha hūṃ phaṭ
As Vajra Vidyā, the supreme king,
the learned one meditates on himself,
a body pervaded by blazing light;
through meditation on the vajra one attains accomplishment.
hūṃ
O Lord of Secrets, the heart mantra of all is thus proclaimed.
Then Vajradhara contemplated the lord of yoga:
utterly seizing those who are not to be tamed, with a loving mind toward those to be tamed,
the supreme lord of mountains, the ground where the trainings play.
The sūkṣma is complete.

The secret is the stars, the Sun and the Moon;
one should recite the vajra pledge.
Beautiful as it is imagined by conceptual thought,
next the rite of offering is explained.
Oneself in the form of the Buddha,
meditate intently on the Buddha's magical emanations.
As was explained in the first application,
one should worship all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata vajra karmeśvari hūṃ
At one's own heart, the mind of awakening;
having imagined the host of all the buddhas,
with this supreme offering
one should worship all the guides.
hūṃ
All the gods, together with Maheśvara,
should be summoned,
and oneself, as Vajra Wrath,
worships them intently with wrathful offerings.
oṃ sarva tathāgata pūjā karma samaya visti vistvar trailokya vijayeśva sarva duṣṭāna mahā samaya vajrī hūṃ
I myself am wholly Bhṛkuṭi;
one should meditate as the jewel wrathful one.
Making all the buddhas afraid,
one worships them intently with jewel offerings.
oṃ sarva tathāgata kapāśa samaya spharaṇa mahā pūjā ākarṣaya vidhi vistvara samaya hūṃ
Appearing equal to the expanse of reality,
I am the steadfast Vajradharma.
Worshipping intently with the offering of Dharma,
one should worship all the buddhas.
oṃ sarva tathāgata dharma dhātu spharaṇa mahā pūjā vidhi vistvara samaya hūṃ
Having become Vajra Karma oneself,
one should meditate on the Buddha's magical emanations.
One should worship all the buddhas,
worshipping them intently with the offering of action.
oṃ sarva tathāgata loka dhātu jīvita mahā karma pūjā vedhi vistvara samaye hūṃ
I am Vajrapāṇi, I am the vajra;
the tathāgatas should be summoned.
Worshipping Vajrasattva intently,
one should meditate on it as an offering.
oṃ vajra satva siddhi jñāna samaye hūṃ jaḥ
As Vajra Hook, supreme among the steadfast,
one should always meditate on oneself.
In order to accomplish the meditation,
one should summon all the buddhas.
oṃ vajra ākarṣa jñāna samaya hūṃ jaḥ
Being oneself Vajrarāga,
one should delight those who have been summoned.
Worshipping intently through Vajrarāga,
apply it properly, thinking, "They are to be accomplished."
oṃ vajra rati rāga samaye hūṃ jaḥ
Meditating on oneself as Vajrasādhu,
one should please the Victors.
Worshipping intently through Vajrasādhu,
one should summon all the buddhas.
oṃ vajra sādhu karma jñāna samaye hūṃ jaḥ
As Vajraratna, the great king,
one should meditate on oneself.
Bringing the tathāgatas under one's power,
rain down a shower of jewels everywhere.
oṃ vajra bhṛkuṭī vaśī kuru hūṃ
Meditate on oneself as the vajra Sun,
endowed with garlands of light rays.
Bringing the buddhas under one's power,
emanate light rays in all directions.
oṃ vajra sūrya maṇḍala vaśīṃ kuru hūṃ
Bringing the tathāgatas under one's power,
I am the very king of the lotus.
Fulfill all the wishes of sentient beings,
and worship intently with the jewel banner.
oṃ vajra dhvajāgra keyūre vaśīṃ kuru hūṃ
Laughing aloud with "ha ha,"
being oneself Vajrahāsa,
bring all the buddhas under one's power;
worship them intently with the offering of laughter.
oṃ vajra aṭṭa hāsana vaśīṃ kuru hūṃ
I am the very king of the lotus,
the queen, steadfast and self-arisen.
I shall make the buddhas passionately attached;
worship intently with passion for desire.
oṃ vajra padma rāgaya hūṃ
I myself am the sharp one,
the steadfast perfection of wisdom.
I shall cut through the afflictions
and make the tathāgatas enamored.
oṃ vajra tikṣṇa rāgaye hūṃ
I am the goddess Vajratārā,
who turns the wheel of Dharma.
I shall please the Vajra Bearer;
worship intently with steadfast passion.
oṃ vajra maṇḍal rāga rāgaya hūṃ
The goddess of space who speaks the Dharma
teaches the inexpressible nature.
Rejoicing in the pure Dharma,
